  [dhana-9-11] Dhana Yoga (9th–11th lords)  planets=Ve,Ju
      The 9th lord Venus joins the 11th lord Jupiter — fortune's lord touching the wealth axis. Money arrives through merit, timing or backing rather than grind alone.
  [parivartana-Ju-Ve] Parivartana Yoga (Maha)  planets=Ju,Ve
      Jupiter and Venus sit in each other's signs — a parivartana, or mutual exchange, linking the 2nd and 11th with the 4th and 9th. An exchange fuses two houses: neither can be read without the other, and whatever happens to one shows up in the other. Both lords own auspicious houses, which is the strong form of the exchange: the two areas actively fund each other, and your periods for either planet tend to move both at once.
  [sunapha] Sunapha Yoga  planets=Ve
      Venus occupies the sign after the Moon. Sunapha builds self-made standing: intelligence applied to earning, wealth accumulated by your own effort rather than inherited, and a reputation that grows on results. What Venus governs is where that earning tends to concentrate.

--- personality ---
  headline: Aquarius rising, Aquarius Moon in Shatabhisha, Aquarius Sun — a personality led by Saturn and tested through Ketu.
  strongest=Sa weakest=Ke
  == Your rising sign — how you come across
     [1] Your rising sign (the 'Lagna' — the sign that was coming up over the eastern horizon at the moment you were born) is Aquarius, at 15°28'. The rising sign describes how you come across, the body you live in, and the way you start things. With Aquarius rising, the outward temperament is detached, systemic and unconventionally principled, thinking in groups and futures rather than individuals and moments. Independent to a fault, and warm in a way that surprises people.
     [2] The rising degree also falls in a nakshatra — one of the 27 star-groups the zodiac is divided into, each with its own character — called Shatabhisha, in its 3rd quarter. That nakshatra colours the body and the first impression you make: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine.
     [3] Every sign has a ruling planet, and the ruler of your rising sign — Saturn — is the planet that stands for you personally in the chart. It sits in your 1st house in Aquarius, which is its moolatrikona, the sign where it works most productively. Its overall strength (a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors) comes to 69 out of 100 (strong). Because it sits in the 1st, the centre of gravity of your life leans toward self: that is the agenda that colours everything you attempt, and your sense of who you are is bound up in it.
     [4] Saturn itself falls in the nakshatra Shatabhisha, in its 2nd quarter, whose ruling planet is Rahu. The flavour of that nakshatra is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. Rahu, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Saturn, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [5] For Aquarius rising, Venus is the yogakaraka — it rules your 4th house of home and your 9th house of fortune. Saturn is on your side as ruler of your rising sign (and of your 12th). Jupiter rules your 2nd and 11th, so it accumulates wealth but also carries the 2nd-house health-watch duty; the Moon (your 6th) and Mars (your 3rd and 10th) need to be well placed before their periods reward you.
  == Your Moon — the mind and its moods
     [1] In Vedic astrology the Moon stands for the mind: your moods, your instincts, what you need in order to feel safe. Your Moon is in Aquarius in your 1st house, which is an enemy's sign, where it meets resistance; its overall strength comes to 42 out of 100 (weak). With the Moon in Aquarius, the mind is detached and observational, feeling at one remove and more comfortable with humanity than with individuals. Emotional independence is real, occasionally to the point of remoteness.
     [2] The Moon falls in the nakshatra Shatabhisha, in its 2nd quarter, whose ruling planet is Rahu. The flavour of that nakshatra is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. This is the single most personal signature in your chart: it sets the sequence of your planetary periods (the Vimshottari dasha, the timing system used throughout this app), and it describes how you actually experience your own life from the inside. Rahu, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Moon, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [3] Because the Moon sits in your 1st house, your emotional life revolves around self. That is where you go for comfort, and it is also where you are most easily unsettled.
  == Your Sun — the will and the core self
     [1] The Sun stands for your will, your sense of self, and the things you need to be respected for. Your Sun is in Aquarius in your 1st house, which is the sign of a great enemy, where it struggles to be itself; its overall strength comes to 48 out of 100 (moderate). With the Sun in Aquarius, the will is independent and reforming: your identity rests on principle rather than on approval.
     [2] Because the Sun sits in your 1st house, your identity is staked on self. This is the arena in which you need to matter, and a setback here feels like a failure of self rather than of circumstance.
     [3] The Sun falls in the nakshatra Purva Bhadrapada, in its 2nd quarter, whose ruling planet is Jupiter. The flavour of that nakshatra is: fiery twin-front transformation; intensity behind an austere face. Jupiter, the nakshatra's ruler, is a friend of Sun, so this quality comes through warmly and is easy for others to read.
  == Your strongest and weakest planets
     [1] Each planet in your chart has been scored for overall strength out of 100 — a combination of the sign it is in, the house, the aspects it receives, and a few other classical factors. Saturn is your strongest planet at 69 (strong), sitting in your 1st house in Aquarius. Its qualities are the ones you reach for by default and perform best with under pressure — whatever Saturn stands for is where you are genuinely, reliably competent. The biggest things in its favour are: Dignity: Moolatrikona; House placement (1); Aspect from Jupiter.
     [2] Ketu is your weakest at 12 (afflicted), in your 4th house in Taurus. This is the chart's soft flank: whatever Ketu stands for is where you tend to over-compensate, avoid, or quietly hand off to other people. The biggest things weighing on it are: Dignity: Debilitated (Neecha); Aspect from Mars; Aspect from Rahu.
     [3] A lot of your personality is the negotiation between these two. Growth, for this chart, looks less like adding to Saturn — which needs no help — and more like refusing to let Ketu's weakness set the terms, because that is precisely the area you are most tempted to route around.
  == Atmakaraka — what this life is for
     [1] Sun is your Atmakaraka — literally the 'soul significator', the planet that has travelled furthest through its sign (26°26'), which the Jaimini school of astrology treats as the planet carrying the deepest purpose of the life. In simple terms: whatever Sun stands for — placed in your 1st house in Aquarius — is the lesson this life keeps returning to, in relationships, in work, everywhere. It is less "what you do" and more "what you cannot avoid becoming good at".
     [2] Its Karakamsa — the sign the Atmakaraka occupies in the Navamsa, the ninth-division chart — is Taurus: the inner room where that lesson is actually worked out. A Taurus Karakamsa colours the soul's work as steady, sensual and immovable once settled, built for accumulation and endurance rather than speed. Comfort is sought sincerely and change resisted instinctively.
  == Navamsa — the inner person
     [1] The Navamsa (D-9) is a second chart derived from your birth chart by dividing each sign into nine parts. Tradition reads it for the inner person and for marriage, and treats it as the test of whether what the birth chart promises actually holds. Its rising sign is Aquarius. If the birth chart is how life presents itself, the Navamsa is how you are on the inside once the noise settles — and an Aquarius inner nature is detached, systemic and unconventionally principled, thinking in groups and futures rather than individuals and moments. Independent to a fault, and warm in a way that surprises people. When the outer sign (Aquarius) and inner sign (Aquarius) differ in element, people who only know you casually often misread you; those close to you meet the Navamsa.
  == By the classical six-fold measure (Shadbala)
     [1] Shadbala is the full classical strength calculation — six separate measures (position, direction, time of birth, motion, nature and aspects) added together and expressed in units called rupas, with each planet having a minimum it is expected to reach. By that measure Sun is your strongest planet at 9.16 rupas (141% of its required minimum) and Jupiter the weakest at 5.49 rupas (85%). Note this differs from the quicker overall score above — the six-fold measure weighs birth-time and motion factors the quick score does not; where they disagree, the Shadbala verdict is the classical one.
     [2] Practically: lean on Sun's themes when the stakes are high, and give Jupiter's themes more preparation time than feels necessary.
  == Aspects on your rising sign — who edits the personality
     [1] Planets influence houses they do not sit in by 'aspecting' them — casting their influence across the chart to particular houses. Every planet aspects the 7th house from itself, and Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Rahu and Ketu each have extra special aspects. The following planets aspect your rising sign, and each one edits the personality others meet:
     [2] Jupiter aspects your rising sign from your 9th house — Jupiter's special 5th-house aspect — its blessing on intelligence, good counsel and children.
     [3] Each of these adds its own colour on top of the rising sign itself. If people describe you in ways that do not quite match the rising-sign portrait above, one of these planets is usually why.
  == How you are seen vs how you are (Arudha Lagna)
     [1] The Arudha Lagna is the point in the chart that stands for your public image — how the world perceives you, as distinct from how you actually are. Yours falls in Scorpio, while your actual rising sign is Aquarius. So the public image runs on Scorpio's wavelength — intense, private and penetrating, with feeling that runs deep and is rarely displayed — while the person underneath operates as Aquarius. The gap is not dishonesty; it is simply that your reputation forms around the themes of your 10th house. Knowing which conversations are about the image and which are about you is a lifelong advantage.
  == Yoga signatures in the personality
     [1] A 'yoga' is a named combination of planets that the classical texts single out as producing a specific result. 4 combinations in your chart stamp themselves directly on the character: Budhaditya Yoga, Chandra-Mangala Yoga, Sasa Yoga (Pancha Mahapurusha), Sunapha Yoga.
     [2] Budhaditya Yoga — Sun and Mercury conjoin in the 1st house: sharp administrative intellect and articulate authority.
     [3] Chandra-Mangala Yoga — Moon and Mars conjoin — an earning combination: entrepreneurial drive fused with instinct. Wealth through self-effort, with a temper that needs channels, not suppression.
     [4] Sasa Yoga (Pancha Mahapurusha) — Saturn is in its moolatrikona and occupies the 1st house, one of the four corner houses (a 'kendra') — one of the five Mahapurusha ("great person") combinations. It stamps the whole personality with formidable endurance and organisational authority built slowly and held for a long time. You govern systems, labour and institutions, and outlast flashier rivals. Coldness and a taste for control are the shadow.
     [5] Sunapha Yoga — Venus occupies the sign after the Moon. Sunapha builds self-made standing: intelligence applied to earning, wealth accumulated by your own effort rather than inherited, and a reputation that grows on results. What Venus governs is where that earning tends to concentrate.
     [6] Beneath that sits a resilience signature — Neechabhanga Raja Yoga — the combinations that convert this chart's difficulties into capability rather than damage. They rarely show early; they are what you are left holding after the hard decade.
     [7] Neechabhanga Raja Yoga — Rahu's debilitation is cancelled because its dispositor Mars occupies a kendra from Lagna; and its dispositor stands in a kendra from the Moon; and Ketu, exalted in that very sign's field, occupies a kendra. What begins as the chart's weakest point matures into a source of unusual strength, typically after early-life struggle in that portfolio.
     [8] Neechabhanga Raja Yoga — Ketu's debilitation is cancelled because Moon, exalted in that very sign's field, occupies a kendra; and its dispositor Venus is itself exalted. What begins as the chart's weakest point matures into a source of unusual strength, typically after early-life struggle in that portfolio.
  == Which planets work for you, and which against
     [1] Which planets help you and which hinder you is not fixed — it depends on your rising sign, because the rising sign decides which houses each planet rules. A planet that rules good houses for Aquarius rising works in your favour whatever its natural reputation; one that rules difficult houses works against you even if it is a 'good' planet in general.
     [2] Venus is the yogakaraka for Aquarius rising — the single most helpful planet your rising sign can have, because it rules one of the corner houses and one of the fortunate houses at the same time. Venus — in your 2nd house, where it is exalted, strength 65 out of 100 (strong). Its condition is the best single predictor of how far your personality converts into standing in the world.
     [3] Working for you: Venus — in your 2nd house, where it is exalted, strength 65 out of 100 (strong); Saturn — in your 1st house, in its moolatrikona sign, strength 69 out of 100 (strong). The traits these planets govern are the ones you can lean on without second-guessing, and their main periods (dashas) feel like the personality working as designed.
     [4] Working against you: Jupiter — in your 9th house, in a great enemy's sign, strength 47 out of 100 (moderate); Moon — in your 1st house, in an enemy's sign, strength 42 out of 100 (weak); Mars — in your 1st house, in an enemy's sign, strength 46 out of 100 (moderate). These are not evil planets — they are the ones whose house rulerships pull against your rising sign's interests. Where one of them is strong, you have a talent that repeatedly gets you into trouble; where weak, a blind spot you rarely notice.
     [5] Neutral: Sun — in your 1st house, in a great enemy's sign, strength 48 out of 100 (moderate); Mercury — in your 1st house, in an enemy's sign, strength 54 out of 100 (moderate). These take their colour from whatever they sit with rather than carrying an agenda of their own.

--- houses ---
  == 1st House — Aquarius  occupied=true lord=Sa planets=[Su,Mo,Ma,Me,Sa] aspects=[Ju:5]
     [1] Your 1st house falls in Aquarius. This is the part of the chart that covers self, body, vitality, temperament and the overall direction of life. Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Saturn all sit in it, which makes this one of the main arenas of your life — a lot happens here, and it happens to you directly.
     [2] Aquarius is ruled by Saturn, so Saturn is the ruler of your 1st house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Saturn sits in your 1st house at 11°09' Aquarius, which is its moolatrikona, the sign where it works most productively. It sits in the very house it rules, which is the strongest endorsement a house can get: the ruler is at home, defending its own affairs directly. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 69 out of 100 (strong), so Saturn is in a position to actually deliver what the 1st house stands for, not merely promise it.
     [3] The ruler, Saturn, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Shatabhisha, in its 2nd quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Rahu, and the flavour is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Saturn, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Sun sits here at 26°26' Aquarius. For your Aquarius rising sign, Sun rules your 7th house — marriage — which makes it neither for you nor against you by rulership (functionally neutral). It also rules one of the two houses (the 2nd and 7th) that the tradition watches for health in its main periods, so its periods deserve a little extra care. The Sun on the Lagna gives an unmistakable presence — authority, vitality and a spine that does not bend easily. The body runs hot and the ego runs close to the surface; leadership comes naturally, but so does the tendency to dominate rooms that only needed a participant. Aquarius belongs to a great enemy of Sun, so it struggles to be itself here. Expect what Sun stands for to be taxed — and expect deliberate attention to this planet to pay back more than usual, precisely because it starts from behind.
     [5] Sun falls in the nakshatra Purva Bhadrapada (2nd quarter), ruled by Jupiter, whose flavour is: fiery twin-front transformation; intensity behind an austere face. Jupiter, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Sun, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [6] Moon sits here at 12°13' Aquarius. For your Aquarius rising sign, Moon rules your 6th house — health — which makes it a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). Moon on the Lagna makes the face of the chart emotional, responsive and public-facing. Moods are visible weather; the public likes this person without quite knowing why. Wellbeing tracks the Moon's dignity and phase closely. Aquarius belongs to an enemy of Moon, so it meets resistance here — a guest whose host does not much like it. Results in this house take roughly twice the usual persistence to arrive.
     [7] Moon falls in the nakshatra Shatabhisha (2nd quarter), ruled by Rahu, whose flavour is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Moon, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [8] Mars sits here at 9°02' Aquarius. For your Aquarius rising sign, Mars rules your 3rd and 10th houses — courage and career — which makes it a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). Mars on the Lagna gives heat to the body and edge to the will — athletic, direct, quick to act and quick to scar. Mangal dosha — the Mars placement traditionally checked before marriage — applies here. You do not wait; the life lesson is learning what deserves the weapon and what deserves patience. Aquarius belongs to an enemy of Mars, so it meets resistance here — a guest whose host does not much like it. Results in this house take roughly twice the usual persistence to arrive.
     [9] Mars falls in the nakshatra Shatabhisha (1st quarter), ruled by Rahu, whose flavour is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Mars, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [10] Mercury sits here at 0°28' Aquarius. For your Aquarius rising sign, Mercury rules your 5th and 8th houses — intelligence and longevity — which makes it neither for you nor against you by rulership (functionally neutral). Mercury on the Lagna gives a quick, youthful, wit-forward presence — the eternal student who reads the room in seconds. Health is wiry rather than robust. The danger is cleverness used as armour against depth. Aquarius belongs to an enemy of Mercury, so it meets resistance here — a guest whose host does not much like it. Results in this house take roughly twice the usual persistence to arrive. One refinement: houses can be counted two ways. By whole signs (used above) Mercury is in the 1st, but by the more precise method that measures from the exact house boundaries (Bhava Chalit), it falls in the 12th. Read its concrete, day-to-day results in the 12th house too; its strength and comfort by sign, described above, stay as they are.
     [11] Mercury falls in the nakshatra Dhanishta (3rd quarter), ruled by Mars, whose flavour is: abundant rhythm; music, wealth and adaptive movement. Mars, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Mercury, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [12] Saturn sits here at 11°09' Aquarius. For your Aquarius rising sign, Saturn rules your 1st and 12th houses — self and expenditure — which makes it a planet that works in your favour by the houses it rules (a 'functional benefic'). Saturn on the Lagna sets an old head on young shoulders: seriousness, duty and a life that improves with age like slow-matured stock. Early years feel heavier than peers'. Whatever you build, you build to last. Aquarius is Saturn's moolatrikona, the sign where it works most productively. Results in this house arrive with unusual reliability — confident, steady and duty-minded.
     [13] Saturn falls in the nakshatra Shatabhisha (2nd quarter), ruled by Rahu, whose flavour is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Saturn, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [14] Moon and the house ruler Saturn both sit in nakshatras ruled by Rahu. They are wired to the same source: what they stand for rises and falls together, and Rahu's own main period (dasha) switches this house on twice over.
     [15] Mars and the house ruler Saturn both sit in nakshatras ruled by Rahu. They are wired to the same source: what they stand for rises and falls together, and Rahu's own main period (dasha) switches this house on twice over.
     [16] Moon and Mars both sit in nakshatras ruled by Rahu, which tightens their pairing: they do not merely share a sign, they answer to the same planet, and Rahu's main periods bring both to the surface at once.
     [17] Moon and Saturn both sit in nakshatras ruled by Rahu, which tightens their pairing: they do not merely share a sign, they answer to the same planet, and Rahu's main periods bring both to the surface at once.
     [18] Mars and Saturn both sit in nakshatras ruled by Rahu, which tightens their pairing: they do not merely share a sign, they answer to the same planet, and Rahu's main periods bring both to the surface at once.
     [19] Jupiter aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 9th house in Libra, in a great enemy's sign, retrograde. By nature Jupiter is a gentle planet (a 'benefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). The aspect landing here is Jupiter's special 5th-house aspect — its blessing on intelligence, good counsel and children. Jupiter's drishti on the Lagna is among the finest protections in a chart: an optimistic, ethical temperament, a well-made body, and a habit of landing on one's feet. Weight, literal and metaphorical, is the only excess. The good intent is there, but the planet itself is poorly placed, so the protection is real yet thin, and tends to arrive later than you expect. Note the split, though: gentle by nature but working against your rising sign by the houses it rules, so its blessing here comes with strings attached.
     [20] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a gentle planet — an unusually protected area, and one of the safer parts of your life.
     [21] Sun–Moon fuses will and feeling into one channel — a self-contained, subjective nature born near the new Moon. Decisiveness is high, but the inner counsel-chamber has one voice; you benefit enormously from advisors you actually consult. The two planets are 14.2° apart and their combined strength is only 41 out of 100, so they share a house more than a purpose. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Sun's main periods.
     [22] Sun–Mars is a fire alliance of friends: commanding energy, executive courage, a temper with a short fuse and shorter memory. Superb for leadership under pressure; hard on subordinates and blood pressure alike. The two planets are 17.4° apart and their combined strength is only 39 out of 100, so they share a house more than a purpose. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Sun's main periods.
     [23] Sun–Mercury (Budhaditya) welds intellect to authority — administrative brilliance and articulate command. When Mercury sits deep in combustion, cleverness serves ego; with distance, it advises it. The two planets are 26.0° apart and their combined strength is only 43 out of 100, so they share a house more than a purpose. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Mercury's main periods.
     [24] Sun–Saturn is father and son sharing one room — authority and duty grinding against each other. Early friction with father-figures matures into formidable, sober leadership. Achievement is real; enjoyment of it must be learned separately. The two planets are 15.3° apart and their combined strength is only 51 out of 100, so they share a house more than a purpose. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Saturn's main periods.
     [25] Moon–Mars (Chandra-Mangala) monetises instinct: entrepreneurial reflexes, protective passion, wealth built through decisive emotional intelligence. The same circuitry produces flash-flood tempers — profitable when dammed, destructive when not. The two planets are 3.2° apart and their combined strength is only 48 out of 100, so they share a house more than a purpose. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Mars's main periods.
     [26] Moon–Mercury blends feeling with articulation: persuasive, adaptable, commercially fluent minds. Mercury's enmity toward the Moon shows as overthinking of feelings; journaling and speech turn the churn into product. The two planets are 11.7° apart and their combined strength is only 44 out of 100, so they share a house more than a purpose. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Mercury's main periods.
     [27] Moon–Saturn chills the inner weather — seriousness, emotional self-rationing, a childhood that taught caution early. Vish-yoga by name, but its mature form is emotional professionalism: you can hold others' grief without drowning. At 1.1° apart the two planets are genuinely blended — combined strength 64 out of 100, with Saturn the stronger. Expect the combination to show up steadily rather than dramatically, in proportion to the effort you put into it.
     [28] Mars–Mercury sharpens language into instrument and argument into sport: technical brilliance, debate skill, engineering communication. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Mercury's main periods.
     [29] Mars–Saturn yokes the accelerator to the brake — frustration as a chronic climate, and, matured, the rarest of engines: disciplined aggression. The combination shows up strongly and early in life, and it defines this house rather than merely colouring it.
     [30] Mercury–Saturn slows thought to load-bearing speed: methodical intellect, legal precision, few words with long warranties. Learning may start late or hard and ends deep. Expect the combination to show up steadily rather than dramatically, in proportion to the effort you put into it.
     [31] With 5 planets sharing one house, it works like a committee: Saturn chairs it as the strongest member, the group spans 26.0° and carries a combined strength of 44 out of 100 (weak blend). These planets water each other down more than they cooperate; the house stays busy without ever becoming decisive.
  == 2nd House — Pisces  occupied=true lord=Ju planets=[Ve] aspects=[Ra:5]
     [1] Your 2nd house falls in Pisces. This is the part of the chart that covers wealth, family, speech, food and accumulated resources. Venus sits in it, so the affairs of this house are things you live directly rather than watch from a distance.
     [2] Pisces is ruled by Jupiter, so Jupiter is the ruler of your 2nd house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Jupiter sits in your 9th house at 20°42' Libra, which is the sign of a great enemy, where it struggles to be itself. It is also retrograde (appearing to move backwards in the sky at your birth). Counting from the 2nd house itself, Jupiter stands 8 places along — the 8th from its own house, which is one of the three difficult positions (the 6th, 8th and 12th, called 'dusthana'). The matters of this house are undermined a little from within, and putting them right takes deliberate, sustained effort rather than luck. Because the ruler sits in the 9th, the affairs of your 2nd house get worked out mostly through fortune — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 47 out of 100 (moderate). Jupiter can carry the 2nd house, but results here follow your own effort closely rather than being handed to you.
     [3] The ruler, Jupiter, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Vishakha, in its 1st quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Jupiter, and the flavour is: goal-fixated determination; twin ambitions pursued with fervour. Jupiter sits in a nakshatra it rules itself, so its intent reaches the world undiluted — what it promises, it delivers in its own voice.
     [4] Venus sits here at 9°15' Pisces. For your Aquarius rising sign, Venus rules your 4th and 9th houses — home and fortune — which makes it the single most helpful planet your rising sign can have (the 'yogakaraka'), because it rules one of the supporting houses and one of the fortunate houses at the same time. Venus in the 2nd sweetens wealth and speech alike: money through arts, luxury goods, finance and beauty industries. The voice may literally be musical. Family life is refined; the palate is expensive and knows it. Pisces is the sign where Venus is exalted — its strongest possible position. Everything Venus stands for runs at full, sometimes overwhelming, capacity here; this is one of the power centres of your chart.
     [5] Venus falls in the nakshatra Uttara Bhadrapada (2nd quarter), ruled by Saturn, whose flavour is: deep-sea serenity; wisdom raised from the depths, slow and profound. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Venus, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [6] Rahu aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 10th house in Scorpio, where it is debilitated, retrograde. By nature Rahu is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is neither for you nor against you by rulership (functionally neutral). The aspect landing here is Rahu's special 5th-house aspect — an amplifier of intelligence and of desire. Rahu's aspect on the 2nd creates hunger around wealth, family and speech — accumulation is driven, sometimes through unorthodox or foreign channels, and the voice can persuade past the facts. Family history carries something unresolved. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [7] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.
  == 3rd House — Aries  occupied=false lord=Ma planets=[] aspects=[Ju:7,Sa:3]
     [1] Your 3rd house falls in Aries. This is the part of the chart that covers courage, siblings, communication, skills and self-effort. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Aries is ruled by Mars, so Mars is the ruler of your 3rd house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Mars sits in your 1st house at 9°02' Aquarius, which is an enemy's sign, where it meets resistance. Counting from the 3rd house itself, Mars stands 11 places along — the 11th from its own house, a workable but unremarkable position that neither helps nor hurts. Because the ruler sits in the 1st, the affairs of your 3rd house get worked out mostly through self — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 46 out of 100 (moderate). Mars can carry the 3rd house, but results here follow your own effort closely rather than being handed to you.
     [3] The ruler, Mars, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Shatabhisha, in its 1st quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Rahu, and the flavour is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Mars, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Jupiter aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 9th house in Libra, in a great enemy's sign, retrograde. By nature Jupiter is a gentle planet (a 'benefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Jupiter's case a direct protection. Jupiter's glance on the 3rd raises courage into principle and communication into teaching; skills are pursued for their meaning. Siblings are a source of support rather than rivalry. The good intent is there, but the planet itself is poorly placed, so the protection is real yet thin, and tends to arrive later than you expect. Note the split, though: gentle by nature but working against your rising sign by the houses it rules, so its blessing here comes with strings attached.
     [5] Saturn also aspects this house, from the 1st house in Aquarius, in its moolatrikona sign — a hard planet by nature, and one that works in your favour by the houses it rules. The aspect landing here is Saturn's special 3rd-house aspect — the grinding one, which wears down rather than blocks outright. Saturn's glance on the 3rd converts courage into endurance: you outlast rather than outcharge. Effort is sustained over years and skills are mastered by repetition, while relations with siblings carry distance or duty. A hard planet in a strong position does not merely trouble a house — it also forces you to become competent in its affairs, charging the discipline as its price. Its rulerships soften this considerably — a hard planet that works for your rising sign behaves far better than its reputation.
     [6] The house receives 1 gentle and 1 hard aspect — a contested area whose results swing depending on which of these planets is running its main period (dasha) at the time.
     [7] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 3rd house goes rests on Mars's condition in the 1st and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Mars — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.
  == 4th House — Taurus  occupied=true lord=Ve planets=[Ke] aspects=[Ma:4,Ra:7]
     [1] Your 4th house falls in Taurus. This is the part of the chart that covers home, mother, emotional foundations, property and inner contentment. Ketu sits in it, so the affairs of this house are things you live directly rather than watch from a distance.
     [2] Taurus is ruled by Venus, so Venus is the ruler of your 4th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Venus sits in your 2nd house at 9°15' Pisces, which is the sign where it is exalted — at its very strongest. Counting from the 4th house itself, Venus stands 11 places along — the 11th from its own house, a workable but unremarkable position that neither helps nor hurts. Because the ruler sits in the 2nd, the affairs of your 4th house get worked out mostly through wealth — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 65 out of 100 (strong), so Venus is in a position to actually deliver what the 4th house stands for, not merely promise it.
     [3] The ruler, Venus, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Uttara Bhadrapada, in its 2nd quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Saturn, and the flavour is: deep-sea serenity; wisdom raised from the depths, slow and profound. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Venus, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [4] Ketu sits here at 3°39' Taurus. Ketu rules no sign of its own. It works through the planet that owns the sign it sits in, through the ruler of its star-group (nakshatra), and through whatever it sits beside — a shadow that takes on the colour of its surroundings rather than a house ruler with an agenda of its own. Ketu in the 4th unroots the home: moves, a mother-bond marked by absence or spirituality, comfort found nowhere until found within. Property matters stay unglamorous. The true homeland you seek has no postal code. Taurus is the sign where Ketu is debilitated — its weakest position in the zodiac. Its confidence is undermined from the start and the matters it rules need conscious, repeated shoring up, unless a cancellation (Neechabhanga, checked in the yoga findings) turns the early weakness into eventual strength. Ketu is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first. One refinement: houses can be counted two ways. By whole signs (used above) Ketu is in the 4th, but by the more precise method that measures from the exact house boundaries (Bhava Chalit), it falls in the 3rd. Read its concrete, day-to-day results in the 3rd house too; its strength and comfort by sign, described above, stay as they are.
     [5] Ketu falls in the nakshatra Krittika (3rd quarter), ruled by Sun, whose flavour is: sharp, purifying fire; cuts through falsehood, ambitious and critical. Sun, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural enemy of Ketu, so even a well-placed Ketu has its results filtered through an unsympathetic hand — expect what it promises to arrive altered, later, or at a price.
     [6] Mars aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 1st house in Aquarius, in an enemy's sign. By nature Mars is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). The aspect landing here is Mars's special 4th-house aspect — Mars at close range, and the most disruptive of its three aspects. Mars's aspect on the 4th disturbs the home's peace — a heated domestic atmosphere, property acquired or held through dispute, and a restlessness that resists settling. It does give real capacity with land, machinery and construction. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [7] Rahu also aspects this house, from the 10th house in Scorpio, where it is debilitated, retrograde — a hard planet by nature, and neutral by rulership. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Rahu's case direct and obsessive. Rahu's aspect on the 4th unsettles the foundations — a foreign or unconventional home, restlessness about property, and something unsettled around the mother. Inner peace is a project here, not a starting condition. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [8] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.

Venus's aspect on the 8th brings gain through the partner's or others' resources, an interest in the esoteric side of love, and longevity supported by an appetite for living. Secret attachments are a recurring theme. Because this gentle planet is itself well placed, its aspect is a genuine protection — something you can rely on when this house is tested. And because this is the most helpful planet your rising sign has (the yogakaraka), its aspect is one of the best structural supports in your chart wherever it lands.
     [6] Ketu also aspects this house, from the 4th house in Taurus, where it is debilitated, retrograde — a hard planet by nature, and neutral by rulership. The aspect landing here is Ketu's special 5th-house aspect — a dissolving touch on intelligence and children. Ketu's glance on the 8th is the planet of letting go in the house of endings and transformation: genuine occult aptitude, ease with mortality and transformation, and sudden events that feel fated rather than accidental. Research runs deep. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [7] The house receives 1 gentle and 2 hard aspects — a contested area whose results swing depending on which of these planets is running its main period (dasha) at the time.
     [8] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 8th house goes rests on Mercury's condition in the 1st and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Mercury — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.
  == 9th House — Libra  occupied=true lord=Ve planets=[Ju] aspects=[]
     [1] Your 9th house falls in Libra. This is the part of the chart that covers fortune, dharma, higher learning, the guru, father and long journeys. Jupiter sits in it, so the affairs of this house are things you live directly rather than watch from a distance.
     [2] Libra is ruled by Venus, so Venus is the ruler of your 9th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Venus sits in your 2nd house at 9°15' Pisces, which is the sign where it is exalted — at its very strongest. Counting from the 9th house itself, Venus stands 6 places along — the 6th from its own house, which is one of the three difficult positions (the 6th, 8th and 12th, called 'dusthana'). The matters of this house are undermined a little from within, and putting them right takes deliberate, sustained effort rather than luck. Because the ruler sits in the 2nd, the affairs of your 9th house get worked out mostly through wealth — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 65 out of 100 (strong), so Venus is in a position to actually deliver what the 9th house stands for, not merely promise it.
     [3] The ruler, Venus, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Uttara Bhadrapada, in its 2nd quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Saturn, and the flavour is: deep-sea serenity; wisdom raised from the depths, slow and profound. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Venus, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [4] Jupiter sits here at 20°42' Libra. For your Aquarius rising sign, Jupiter rules your 2nd and 11th houses — wealth and gains — which makes it a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). It also rules one of the two houses (the 2nd and 7th) that the tradition watches for health in its main periods, so its periods deserve a little extra care. Jupiter in the 9th is the guru on the guru's throne: faith, fortune, a benevolent father, and luck that looks unearned to onlookers. Higher learning, pilgrimage and publishing prosper. Your beliefs shape your entire material outcome. Libra belongs to a great enemy of Jupiter, so it struggles to be itself here. Expect what Jupiter stands for to be taxed — and expect deliberate attention to this planet to pay back more than usual, precisely because it starts from behind. Jupiter is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first.
     [5] Jupiter falls in the nakshatra Vishakha (1st quarter), ruled by Jupiter, whose flavour is: goal-fixated determination; twin ambitions pursued with fervour. Jupiter sits in a nakshatra it rules itself, so its intent reaches the world undiluted — what it promises, it delivers in its own voice.
     [6] No planet aspects the 9th house. Its affairs run on the ruler's condition alone, with nothing from outside either reinforcing or interfering — outcomes here are quieter and more in your own hands than elsewhere in the chart.
  == 10th House — Scorpio  occupied=true lord=Ma planets=[Ra] aspects=[Sa:10,Ke:7]
     [1] Your 10th house falls in Scorpio. This is the part of the chart that covers career, status, authority, karma and public achievement. Rahu sits in it, so the affairs of this house are things you live directly rather than watch from a distance.
     [2] Scorpio is ruled by Mars, so Mars is the ruler of your 10th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Mars sits in your 1st house at 9°02' Aquarius, which is an enemy's sign, where it meets resistance. Counting from the 10th house itself, Mars stands 4 places along — the 4th from its own house, which is one of the supportive positions (the corner houses 1, 4, 7, 10 and the fortunate houses 5 and 9). The ruler actively backs what it rules. Because the ruler sits in the 1st, the affairs of your 10th house get worked out mostly through self — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 46 out of 100 (moderate). Mars can carry the 10th house, but results here follow your own effort closely rather than being handed to you.
     [3] The ruler, Mars, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Shatabhisha, in its 1st quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Rahu, and the flavour is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Mars, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Rahu sits here at 3°39' Scorpio. Rahu rules no sign of its own. It works through the planet that owns the sign it sits in, through the ruler of its star-group (nakshatra), and through whatever it sits beside — a shadow that takes on the colour of its surroundings rather than a house ruler with an agenda of its own. Rahu in the 10th is the fame algorithm: obsessive career ambition, mass-market instincts, success through technology, media or foreign systems. Reputation inflates fast — and needs ethical ballast to avoid equally fast correction. Scorpio is the sign where Rahu is debilitated — its weakest position in the zodiac. Its confidence is undermined from the start and the matters it rules need conscious, repeated shoring up, unless a cancellation (Neechabhanga, checked in the yoga findings) turns the early weakness into eventual strength. Rahu is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first. One refinement: houses can be counted two ways. By whole signs (used above) Rahu is in the 10th, but by the more precise method that measures from the exact house boundaries (Bhava Chalit), it falls in the 9th. Read its concrete, day-to-day results in the 9th house too; its strength and comfort by sign, described above, stay as they are.
     [5] Rahu falls in the nakshatra Anuradha (1st quarter), ruled by Saturn, whose flavour is: loyal, disciplined devotion; friendship as a spiritual force. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Rahu, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [6] Saturn aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 1st house in Aquarius, in its moolatrikona sign. By nature Saturn is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet that works in your favour by the houses it rules (a 'functional benefic'). The aspect landing here is Saturn's special 10th-house aspect — the aspect of duty imposed and work scrutinised. Saturn's aspect on the 10th lands on the house Saturn naturally stands for, and is one of the strongest career glances in the chart: authority through service, seniority through endurance, and a reputation for reliability that compounds. Advancement is slow and essentially irreversible. A hard planet in a strong position does not merely trouble a house — it also forces you to become competent in its affairs, charging the discipline as its price. Its rulerships soften this considerably — a hard planet that works for your rising sign behaves far better than its reputation.
     [7] Ketu also aspects this house, from the 4th house in Taurus, where it is debilitated, retrograde — a hard planet by nature, and neutral by rulership. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Ketu's case direct detachment. Ketu on the 10th gives a career pursued without hunger for its rewards, often technical, healing or spiritual, and a public standing you are oddly indifferent to. Ambition arrives late, if at all, and the work is better for it. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [8] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.

  == What tends to take you there
     [1] The foreign houses in your chart point at work — a posting, a transfer, or a job that only exists somewhere else; study or teaching — going to learn something, or to pass it on; a marriage or a business partnership that takes you abroad. That is usually the thread worth following when an opportunity appears and you are trying to judge whether it is the one.
  == Direction — the softest reading here
     [1] Tradition reads a direction from your strongest foreign significator, which is Saturn: West of your birthplace. Hold this one loosely. Direction rules are the weakest technique on this page, and no sensible decision about where to live should turn on them — if a good opportunity points the other way, take the opportunity.

--- section: cautions ---
  headline: 9 areas in your chart deserve conscious handling rather than luck. Each one below comes with something practical to do about it, and none of them is a verdict.
  score=33 verdict=none confidence=75
  == How to read this
     [1] These are tendencies, not sentences, and none of them is a verdict on your life. Classical astrology lists them for one reason: so you can steer. That is why every caution below arrives with the thing that actually helps — a caution without a counter-measure is just anxiety with a citation.
     [2] Nothing here is medical, legal or financial advice, and none of it should stand in for a professional who can look at your actual situation.

--- section: lucky ---
  headline: The planets working in your favour are Saturn, Venus, Rahu — which gives you numbers 4, 8, 6, the colour smoky grey, and South-East to face.
  score=none verdict=none confidence=72
  == Where these come from
     [1] From your chart: the ruler of your rising sign (Saturn), your yogakaraka (Venus), the ruler of your Moon's nakshatra (Rahu) and your strongest supportive planet are the ones working in your favour. Their numbers, colours, days and directions are what this section calls yours.
     [2] From your birth date: Moolank 2 and Bhagyank 1, and a name number of 4 on the chaldean system, with a Kua number of 9 — each of which tradition assigns to a planet.
     [3] Treat all of this the way tradition actually intends it: as a set of preferences that tilt the odds slightly and cost nothing to follow, not as rules that decide outcomes. Wearing your colour to an interview is worth about as much as the confidence it gives you, which is not nothing.

--- yogas ---
  [nbrj-Su] Neechabhanga Raja Yoga  planets=Su
      Sun's debilitation is cancelled because Saturn, exalted in that very sign's field, occupies a kendra. What begins as the chart's weakest point matures into a source of unusual strength, typically after early-life struggle in that portfolio.
  [nbrj-Mo] Neechabhanga Raja Yoga  planets=Mo
      Moon's debilitation is cancelled because its dispositor Mars occupies a kendra from Lagna; and its dispositor stands in a kendra from the Moon. What begins as the chart's weakest point matures into a source of unusual strength, typically after early-life struggle in that portfolio.
  [vrj-Ve] Vipareeta Raja Yoga (Harsha)  planets=Ve
      Venus, lord of the 6th, sits in the 6th — the ruler of a difficult house (the 6th, 8th and 12th are the "dusthana" houses) sitting in another difficult house, which the tradition reads as a reversal. Adversity turns on itself: rivals self-destruct, crises resolve in your favour, and gains arrive through difficulty others cannot stomach.
  [chandra-mangala] Chandra-Mangala Yoga  planets=Mo,Ma
      Moon and Mars conjoin — an earning combination: entrepreneurial drive fused with instinct. Wealth through self-effort, with a temper that needs channels, not suppression.
  [mahapurusha-Ma] Ruchaka Yoga (Pancha Mahapurusha)  planets=Ma
      Mars is in its own sign and occupies the 7th house, one of the four corner houses (a 'kendra') — one of the five Mahapurusha ("great person") combinations. It stamps the whole personality with a commanding, athletic physicality and fearless executive nerve. You lead from the front, thrive in competition, defence, surgery or engineering, and carry authority that is taken rather than granted. Anger is the tax on the gift.
  [yk-Sa] Yogakaraka in Strength  planets=Sa
      Saturn, the most helpful planet for your rising sign (the 'yogakaraka' — it rules a corner house and a fortunate house at once), occupies the 4th house. A single-planet engine of rank: its main periods (dashas) carry the chart's biggest promotions in status and material standing.
  [raja-Ma-Me] Raja Yoga (Kendra–Trikona link)  planets=Ma,Me
      Mars (an angle lord) and Mercury (a trine lord) conjoin. The classical engine of rank: when the ruler of a fortune house (1st, 5th or 9th) joins the ruler of a corner house (1st, 4th, 7th or 10th), status is lifted, and the results ripen in the main periods (dashas) of the two planets involved.
  [anapha] Anapha Yoga  planets=Ve
      Venus occupies the sign before the Moon. Anapha gives a settled constitution and an agreeable presence: health that holds, comfort that is enjoyed rather than chased, and a good name that arrives without campaigning for it. A pull toward simplicity often shows in the second half of life.

--- personality ---
  headline: Taurus rising, Scorpio Moon in Anuradha, Libra Sun — a personality led by Jupiter and tested through Sun.
  strongest=Ju weakest=Su
  == Your rising sign — how you come across
     [1] Your rising sign (the 'Lagna' — the sign that was coming up over the eastern horizon at the moment you were born) is Taurus, at 8°01'. The rising sign describes how you come across, the body you live in, and the way you start things. With Taurus rising, the outward temperament is steady, sensual and immovable once settled, built for accumulation and endurance rather than speed. Comfort is sought sincerely and change resisted instinctively.
     [2] The rising degree also falls in a nakshatra — one of the 27 star-groups the zodiac is divided into, each with its own character — called Krittika, in its 4th quarter. That nakshatra colours the body and the first impression you make: sharp, purifying fire; cuts through falsehood, ambitious and critical.
     [3] Every sign has a ruling planet, and the ruler of your rising sign — Venus — is the planet that stands for you personally in the chart. It sits in your 6th house in Libra, which is its own sign, so it is on home ground; it is also retrograde, which turns its energy inward. Its overall strength (a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors) comes to 53 out of 100 (moderate). Because it sits in the 6th, the centre of gravity of your life leans toward health: that is the agenda that colours everything you attempt, and your sense of who you are is bound up in it.
     [4] Venus itself falls in the nakshatra Vishakha, in its 2nd quarter, whose ruling planet is Jupiter. The flavour of that nakshatra is: goal-fixated determination; twin ambitions pursued with fervour. Jupiter, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Venus, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [5] For Taurus rising, Saturn is the textbook yogakaraka — it rules both your 9th house of fortune and your 10th house of career, so its strong periods build career and fortune together. Jupiter (your 8th and 11th) and the Moon (your 3rd) work against the grain for this rising sign. Mars, as ruler of your 7th, is one of the two planets the tradition watches for health in its periods.
  == Your Moon — the mind and its moods
     [1] In Vedic astrology the Moon stands for the mind: your moods, your instincts, what you need in order to feel safe. Your Moon is in Scorpio in your 7th house, which is the sign where it is debilitated — its weakest position; its overall strength comes to 23 out of 100 (afflicted). With the Moon in Scorpio, the mind runs deep, secretive and intense, with feelings neither displayed nor discarded. This is the Moon's hardest station and the compensation is formidable psychological depth.
     [2] The Moon falls in the nakshatra Anuradha, in its 2nd quarter, whose ruling planet is Saturn. The flavour of that nakshatra is: loyal, disciplined devotion; friendship as a spiritual force. This is the single most personal signature in your chart: it sets the sequence of your planetary periods (the Vimshottari dasha, the timing system used throughout this app), and it describes how you actually experience your own life from the inside. Saturn, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Moon, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [3] Because the Moon sits in your 7th house, your emotional life revolves around marriage. That is where you go for comfort, and it is also where you are most easily unsettled.
  == Your Sun — the will and the core self
     [1] The Sun stands for your will, your sense of self, and the things you need to be respected for. Your Sun is in Libra in your 6th house, which is the sign where it is debilitated — its weakest position; its overall strength comes to 11 out of 100 (afflicted). With the Sun in Libra, the will is relational and consultative: your identity is negotiated rather than asserted, which costs the Sun its natural authority but produces genuine fairness.
     [2] Because the Sun sits in your 6th house, your identity is staked on health. This is the arena in which you need to matter, and a setback here feels like a failure of self rather than of circumstance.
     [3] The Sun falls in the nakshatra Swati, in its 3rd quarter, whose ruling planet is Rahu. The flavour of that nakshatra is: independent, wind-borne flexibility; scatters then finds its own path. Rahu, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Sun, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
  == Your strongest and weakest planets
     [1] Each planet in your chart has been scored for overall strength out of 100 — a combination of the sign it is in, the house, the aspects it receives, and a few other classical factors. Jupiter is your strongest planet at 64 (strong), sitting in your 3rd house in Cancer. Its qualities are the ones you reach for by default and perform best with under pressure — whatever Jupiter stands for is where you are genuinely, reliably competent. The biggest things in its favour are: Dignity: Exalted (Parama Ucha); Ashtakavarga 6 bindus in sign.
     [2] Sun is your weakest at 11 (afflicted), in your 6th house in Libra. This is the chart's soft flank: whatever Sun stands for is where you tend to over-compensate, avoid, or quietly hand off to other people. The biggest things weighing on it are: Dignity: Debilitated (Neecha); House placement (6); Aspect from Saturn.
     [3] A lot of your personality is the negotiation between these two. Growth, for this chart, looks less like adding to Jupiter — which needs no help — and more like refusing to let Sun's weakness set the terms, because that is precisely the area you are most tempted to route around.
  == Atmakaraka — what this life is for
     [1] Venus is your Atmakaraka — literally the 'soul significator', the planet that has travelled furthest through its sign (24°45'), which the Jaimini school of astrology treats as the planet carrying the deepest purpose of the life. In simple terms: whatever Venus stands for — placed in your 6th house in Libra — is the lesson this life keeps returning to, in relationships, in work, everywhere. It is less "what you do" and more "what you cannot avoid becoming good at".
     [2] Its Karakamsa — the sign the Atmakaraka occupies in the Navamsa, the ninth-division chart — is Taurus: the inner room where that lesson is actually worked out. A Taurus Karakamsa colours the soul's work as steady, sensual and immovable once settled, built for accumulation and endurance rather than speed. Comfort is sought sincerely and change resisted instinctively.
  == Navamsa — the inner person
     [1] The Navamsa (D-9) is a second chart derived from your birth chart by dividing each sign into nine parts. Tradition reads it for the inner person and for marriage, and treats it as the test of whether what the birth chart promises actually holds. Its rising sign is Pisces. If the birth chart is how life presents itself, the Navamsa is how you are on the inside once the noise settles — and a Pisces inner nature is impressionable, compassionate and porous to atmosphere, absorbing whatever surrounds you and needing boundaries you are reluctant to build. Imagination and faith are the standing strengths. When the outer sign (Taurus) and inner sign (Pisces) differ in element, people who only know you casually often misread you; those close to you meet the Navamsa.
  == By the classical six-fold measure (Shadbala)
     [1] Shadbala is the full classical strength calculation — six separate measures (position, direction, time of birth, motion, nature and aspects) added together and expressed in units called rupas, with each planet having a minimum it is expected to reach. By that measure Jupiter is your strongest planet at 9.25 rupas (142% of its required minimum) and Venus the weakest at 5.59 rupas (102%). This agrees with the quicker overall score above, which makes the reading more solid.
     [2] Practically: lean on Jupiter's themes when the stakes are high, and give Venus's themes more preparation time than feels necessary.
  == Aspects on your rising sign — who edits the personality
     [1] Planets influence houses they do not sit in by 'aspecting' them — casting their influence across the chart to particular houses. Every planet aspects the 7th house from itself, and Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Rahu and Ketu each have extra special aspects. The following planets aspect your rising sign, and each one edits the personality others meet:
     [2] Moon aspects your rising sign from your 7th house — the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts.
     [3] Mars aspects your rising sign from your 7th house — the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Mars's case direct and unavoidable.
     [4] Mercury aspects your rising sign from your 7th house — the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts.
     [5] Saturn aspects your rising sign from your 4th house — Saturn's special 10th-house aspect — the aspect of duty imposed and work scrutinised.
     [6] Rahu aspects your rising sign from your 5th house — Rahu's special 9th-house aspect — a distorting influence on inherited fortune and belief.
     [7] Each of these adds its own colour on top of the rising sign itself. If people describe you in ways that do not quite match the rising-sign portrait above, one of these planets is usually why.
  == How you are seen vs how you are (Arudha Lagna)
     [1] The Arudha Lagna is the point in the chart that stands for your public image — how the world perceives you, as distinct from how you actually are. Yours falls in Pisces, while your actual rising sign is Taurus. So the public image runs on Pisces's wavelength — impressionable, compassionate and porous to atmosphere, absorbing whatever surrounds you and needing boundaries you are reluctant to build — while the person underneath operates as Taurus. The gap is not dishonesty; it is simply that your reputation forms around the themes of your 11th house. Knowing which conversations are about the image and which are about you is a lifelong advantage.
  == Yoga signatures in the personality
     [1] A 'yoga' is a named combination of planets that the classical texts single out as producing a specific result. 4 combinations in your chart stamp themselves directly on the character: Chandra-Mangala Yoga, Ruchaka Yoga (Pancha Mahapurusha), Yogakaraka in Strength, Anapha Yoga.
     [2] Chandra-Mangala Yoga — Moon and Mars conjoin — an earning combination: entrepreneurial drive fused with instinct. Wealth through self-effort, with a temper that needs channels, not suppression.
     [3] Ruchaka Yoga (Pancha Mahapurusha) — Mars is in its own sign and occupies the 7th house, one of the four corner houses (a 'kendra') — one of the five Mahapurusha ("great person") combinations. It stamps the whole personality with a commanding, athletic physicality and fearless executive nerve. You lead from the front, thrive in competition, defence, surgery or engineering, and carry authority that is taken rather than granted. Anger is the tax on the gift.
     [4] Yogakaraka in Strength — Saturn, the most helpful planet for your rising sign (the 'yogakaraka' — it rules a corner house and a fortunate house at once), occupies the 4th house. A single-planet engine of rank: its main periods (dashas) carry the chart's biggest promotions in status and material standing.
     [5] Anapha Yoga — Venus occupies the sign before the Moon. Anapha gives a settled constitution and an agreeable presence: health that holds, comfort that is enjoyed rather than chased, and a good name that arrives without campaigning for it. A pull toward simplicity often shows in the second half of life.
     [6] Beneath that sits a resilience signature — Neechabhanga Raja Yoga, Vipareeta Raja Yoga (Harsha) — the combinations that convert this chart's difficulties into capability rather than damage. They rarely show early; they are what you are left holding after the hard decade.
     [7] Neechabhanga Raja Yoga — Sun's debilitation is cancelled because Saturn, exalted in that very sign's field, occupies a kendra. What begins as the chart's weakest point matures into a source of unusual strength, typically after early-life struggle in that portfolio.
     [8] Neechabhanga Raja Yoga — Moon's debilitation is cancelled because its dispositor Mars occupies a kendra from Lagna; and its dispositor stands in a kendra from the Moon. What begins as the chart's weakest point matures into a source of unusual strength, typically after early-life struggle in that portfolio.
     [9] Vipareeta Raja Yoga (Harsha) — Venus, lord of the 6th, sits in the 6th — the ruler of a difficult house (the 6th, 8th and 12th are the "dusthana" houses) sitting in another difficult house, which the tradition reads as a reversal. Adversity turns on itself: rivals self-destruct, crises resolve in your favour, and gains arrive through difficulty others cannot stomach.
  == Which planets work for you, and which against
     [1] Which planets help you and which hinder you is not fixed — it depends on your rising sign, because the rising sign decides which houses each planet rules. A planet that rules good houses for Taurus rising works in your favour whatever its natural reputation; one that rules difficult houses works against you even if it is a 'good' planet in general.
     [2] Saturn is the yogakaraka for Taurus rising — the single most helpful planet your rising sign can have, because it rules one of the corner houses and one of the fortunate houses at the same time. Saturn — in your 4th house, in a sign neutral to it, strength 52 out of 100 (moderate). Its condition is the best single predictor of how far your personality converts into standing in the world.
     [3] Working for you: Saturn — in your 4th house, in a sign neutral to it, strength 52 out of 100 (moderate); Mercury — in your 7th house, in an enemy's sign, strength 34 out of 100 (weak); Sun — in your 6th house, where it is debilitated, strength 11 out of 100 (afflicted). The traits these planets govern are the ones you can lean on without second-guessing, and their main periods (dashas) feel like the personality working as designed.
     [4] Working against you: Moon — in your 7th house, where it is debilitated, strength 23 out of 100 (afflicted); Jupiter — in your 3rd house, where it is exalted, strength 64 out of 100 (strong); Mars — in your 7th house, in its own sign, strength 58 out of 100 (moderate). These are not evil planets — they are the ones whose house rulerships pull against your rising sign's interests. Where one of them is strong, you have a talent that repeatedly gets you into trouble; where weak, a blind spot you rarely notice.
     [5] Neutral: Venus — in your 6th house, in its own sign, strength 53 out of 100 (moderate). These take their colour from whatever they sit with rather than carrying an agenda of their own.

--- houses ---
  == 1st House — Taurus  occupied=false lord=Ve planets=[] aspects=[Mo:7,Ma:7,Me:7,Sa:10,Ra:9]
     [1] Your 1st house falls in Taurus. This is the part of the chart that covers self, body, vitality, temperament and the overall direction of life. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Taurus is ruled by Venus, so Venus is the ruler of your 1st house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Venus sits in your 6th house at 24°45' Libra, which is its own sign, so it is on home ground. It is also retrograde (appearing to move backwards in the sky at your birth). Counting from the 1st house itself, Venus stands 6 places along — the 6th from its own house, which is one of the three difficult positions (the 6th, 8th and 12th, called 'dusthana'). The matters of this house are undermined a little from within, and putting them right takes deliberate, sustained effort rather than luck. Because the ruler sits in the 6th, the affairs of your 1st house get worked out mostly through health — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 53 out of 100 (moderate). Venus can carry the 1st house, but results here follow your own effort closely rather than being handed to you.
     [3] The ruler, Venus, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Vishakha, in its 2nd quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Jupiter, and the flavour is: goal-fixated determination; twin ambitions pursued with fervour. Jupiter, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Venus, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Moon aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 7th house in Scorpio, where it is debilitated. By nature Moon is a gentle planet (a 'benefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts. The Moon's gaze on the Lagna gives an approachable, responsive temperament and a face people find easy to trust; the body and the mood, however, run on tides rather than on a schedule. The good intent is there, but the planet itself is poorly placed, so the protection is real yet thin, and tends to arrive later than you expect. Note the split, though: gentle by nature but working against your rising sign by the houses it rules, so its blessing here comes with strings attached.
     [5] Mars also aspects this house, from the 7th house in Scorpio, in its own sign — a hard planet by nature, and one that pulls against your interests by the houses it rules. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Mars's case direct and unavoidable. Mars's drishti on the Lagna gives physical courage, a competitive edge and a body built for exertion, along with a temper that arrives before the reasoning does. Scars, literal and otherwise, are collected early. A hard planet in a strong position does not merely trouble a house — it also forces you to become competent in its affairs, charging the discipline as its price.
     [6] Mercury also aspects this house, from the 7th house in Scorpio, in an enemy's sign — a hard planet by nature, and one that works in your favour by the houses it rules. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts. Mercury's gaze on the Lagna gives a youthful, quick-witted presence and a nervous system that runs on information; you are articulate long before you are settled. Adaptability is the strength, dispersion the risk. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline. Its rulerships soften this considerably — a hard planet that works for your rising sign behaves far better than its reputation.
     [7] Saturn also aspects this house, from the 4th house in Leo, in a sign neutral to it — a hard planet by nature, and your rising sign's most helpful planet (the yogakaraka). The aspect landing here is Saturn's special 10th-house aspect — the aspect of duty imposed and work scrutinised. Saturn's drishti on the Lagna sobers the temperament and slows the body's early flowering; you seem older than your years and come into your own late. What is built on this foundation does not fall down. Coming from a hard planet in an ordinary condition, the aspect keeps this house honest — pressure you learn to work with rather than damage. And because this is the most helpful planet your rising sign has (the yogakaraka), its aspect is one of the best structural supports in your chart wherever it lands.
     [8] Rahu also aspects this house, from the 5th house in Virgo, in a great friend's sign, retrograde — a hard planet by nature, and neutral by rulership. The aspect landing here is Rahu's special 9th-house aspect — a distorting influence on inherited fortune and belief. Rahu's drishti on the Lagna amplifies the persona and blurs its edges: unusual magnetism, an unconventional self-presentation, and an identity assembled rather than inherited. Other people's projections stick to you easily. A hard planet in a strong position does not merely trouble a house — it also forces you to become competent in its affairs, charging the discipline as its price.
     [9] The house receives 1 gentle and 4 hard aspects — a contested area whose results swing depending on which of these planets is running its main period (dasha) at the time.
     [10] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 1st house goes rests on Venus's condition in the 6th and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Venus — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.
  == 2nd House — Gemini  occupied=false lord=Me planets=[] aspects=[Ma:8]
     [1] Your 2nd house falls in Gemini. This is the part of the chart that covers wealth, family, speech, food and accumulated resources. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Gemini is ruled by Mercury, so Mercury is the ruler of your 2nd house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Mercury sits in your 7th house at 5°13' Scorpio, which is an enemy's sign, where it meets resistance. Counting from the 2nd house itself, Mercury stands 6 places along — the 6th from its own house, which is one of the three difficult positions (the 6th, 8th and 12th, called 'dusthana'). The matters of this house are undermined a little from within, and putting them right takes deliberate, sustained effort rather than luck. Because the ruler sits in the 7th, the affairs of your 2nd house get worked out mostly through marriage — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 34 out of 100 (weak), which is low.      [3] The ruler, Mercury, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Anuradha, in its 1st quarter.      [4] Mars aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 7th house in Scorpio, in its own sign.
