Mars's aspect on the 2nd sharpens speech into a weapon and makes wealth something to be fought for and defended rather than inherited. Family relations carry friction; the tongue needs a guard rail. A hard planet in a strong position does not merely trouble a house — it also forces you to become competent in its affairs, charging the discipline as its price.
     [5] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.
     [6] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 2nd house goes rests on Mercury's condition in the 7th and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Mercury — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.
  == 3rd House — Cancer  occupied=true lord=Mo planets=[Ju] aspects=[Ke:5]
     [1] Your 3rd house falls in Cancer. This is the part of the chart that covers courage, siblings, communication, skills and self-effort. Jupiter sits in it, so the affairs of this house are things you live directly rather than watch from a distance.
     [2] Cancer is ruled by Moon, so Moon is the ruler of your 3rd house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Counting from the 3rd house itself, Moon stands 5 places along — the 5th from its own house, which is one of the supportive positions (the corner houses 1, 4, 7, 10 and the fortunate houses 5 and 9). The ruler actively backs what it rules. Because the ruler sits in the 7th, the affairs of your 3rd house get worked out mostly through marriage — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 23 out of 100 (afflicted), which is low. The 3rd house is under-resourced at its ruler, and its affairs need conscious support from you before they hold weight.
     [3] The ruler, Moon, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Anuradha, in its 2nd quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Saturn, and the flavour is: loyal, disciplined devotion; friendship as a spiritual force. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Moon, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Jupiter sits here at 14°37' Cancer. For your Taurus rising sign, Jupiter rules your 8th and 11th houses — longevity and gains — which makes it a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). Jupiter in the 3rd gives wise initiative — courage tempered by ethics. Siblings prosper or are themselves guru-figures. Writing and teaching flow naturally, though ambition here is softer than talent; effort must be deliberate. Cancer is the sign where Jupiter is exalted — its strongest possible position. Everything Jupiter stands for runs at full, sometimes overwhelming, capacity here; this is one of the power centres of your chart.
     [5] Jupiter falls in the nakshatra Pushya (4th quarter), ruled by Saturn, whose flavour is: nourishing, dutiful, deeply auspicious; grows what it protects. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Jupiter, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [6] Jupiter and the house ruler Moon both sit in nakshatras ruled by Saturn. They are wired to the same source: what they stand for rises and falls together, and Saturn's own main period (dasha) switches this house on twice over.
     [7] Ketu aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 11th house in Pisces, in an enemy's sign, retrograde. By nature Ketu is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is neither for you nor against you by rulership (functionally neutral). The aspect landing here is Ketu's special 5th-house aspect — a dissolving touch on intelligence and children. Ketu's glance on the 3rd makes courage sporadic but instinctive: you act decisively in flashes and disengage between them. Skills are picked up intuitively; sibling ties are karmically loose. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [8] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.
  == 4th House — Leo  occupied=true lord=Su planets=[Sa] aspects=[]
     [1] Your 4th house falls in Leo. This is the part of the chart that covers home, mother, emotional foundations, property and inner contentment. Saturn sits in it, so the affairs of this house are things you live directly rather than watch from a distance.
     [2] Leo is ruled by Sun, so Sun is the ruler of your 4th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Sun sits in your 6th house at 16°05' Libra, which is the sign where it is debilitated — its weakest position. Counting from the 4th house itself, Sun stands 3 places along — the 3rd from its own house, a workable but unremarkable position that neither helps nor hurts. Because the ruler sits in the 6th, the affairs of your 4th house get worked out mostly through health — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 11 out of 100 (afflicted), which is low. The 4th house is under-resourced at its ruler, and its affairs need conscious support from you before they hold weight.
     [3] The ruler, Sun, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Swati, in its 3rd quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Rahu, and the flavour is: independent, wind-borne flexibility; scatters then finds its own path. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Sun, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Saturn sits here at 17°59' Leo. For your Taurus rising sign, Saturn rules your 9th and 10th houses — fortune and career — which makes it the single most helpful planet your rising sign can have (the 'yogakaraka'), because it rules one of the supporting houses and one of the fortunate houses at the same time. Saturn in the 4th chills the hearth: emotional reserve at home, a mother marked by duty or hardship, property acquired late or through labour. Inner peace is a discipline, not a default — and once practised, unshakeable. Leo is neutral ground for Saturn — the sign neither helps nor hinders it. What you get out of this house tracks what you put in almost exactly, with no subsidy and no surcharge.
     [5] Saturn falls in the nakshatra Purva Phalguni (2nd quarter), ruled by Venus, whose flavour is: pleasure-loving warmth; charm, patronage and creative enjoyment. Venus, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Saturn, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [6] No planet aspects the 4th house. Its affairs run on the ruler's condition alone, with nothing from outside either reinforcing or interfering — outcomes here are quieter and more in your own hands than elsewhere in the chart.
  == 5th House — Virgo  occupied=true lord=Me planets=[Ra] aspects=[Ke:7]
     [1] Your 5th house falls in Virgo. This is the part of the chart that covers intelligence, children, creativity, romance and purva-punya (past merit). Rahu sits in it, so the affairs of this house are things you live directly rather than watch from a distance.
     [2] Virgo is ruled by Mercury, so Mercury is the ruler of your 5th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Mercury sits in your 7th house at 5°13' Scorpio, which is an enemy's sign, where it meets resistance. Counting from the 5th house itself, Mercury stands 3 places along — the 3rd from its own house, a workable but unremarkable position that neither helps nor hurts. Because the ruler sits in the 7th, the affairs of your 5th house get worked out mostly through marriage — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 34 out of 100 (weak), which is low. The 5th house is under-resourced at its ruler, and its affairs need conscious support from you before they hold weight.
     [3] The ruler, Mercury, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Anuradha, in its 1st quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Saturn, and the flavour is: loyal, disciplined devotion; friendship as a spiritual force. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Mercury, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Rahu sits here at 0°49' Virgo. Rahu rules no sign of its own. It works through the planet that owns the sign it sits in, through the ruler of its star-group (nakshatra), and through whatever it sits beside — a shadow that takes on the colour of its surroundings rather than a house ruler with an agenda of its own. Rahu in the 5th intoxicates intellect and romance: unconventional love, gifted but complex children, speculative genius that must be caged in rules. Political instinct is strong. Mantra practice tames what analysis cannot. Virgo belongs to a great friend of Rahu, so it is warmly hosted here. Its promises are delivered with goodwill, and usually with a little extra. Rahu is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first. One refinement: houses can be counted two ways. By whole signs (used above) Rahu is in the 5th, but by the more precise method that measures from the exact house boundaries (Bhava Chalit), it falls in the 4th. Read its concrete, day-to-day results in the 4th house too; its strength and comfort by sign, described above, stay as they are.
     [5] Rahu falls in the nakshatra Uttara Phalguni (2nd quarter), ruled by Sun, whose flavour is: contractual grace; balances generosity with prudent alliance. Sun, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural enemy of Rahu, so even a well-placed Rahu has its results filtered through an unsympathetic hand — expect what it promises to arrive altered, later, or at a price.
     [6] Ketu aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 11th house in Pisces, in an enemy's sign, retrograde. By nature Ketu is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is neither for you nor against you by rulership (functionally neutral). The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Ketu's case direct detachment. Ketu's glance on the 5th gives intuitive, non-linear intelligence and a spiritual bent to creativity, alongside karmic complications around children or a romance that dissolves rather than concludes. Mantra and technical study both come easily. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline.
     [7] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.

  == 8th House — Sagittarius  occupied=false lord=Ju planets=[] aspects=[]
     [1] Your 8th house falls in Sagittarius. This is the part of the chart that covers longevity, transformation, inheritance, occult depth and sudden events. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Sagittarius is ruled by Jupiter, so Jupiter is the ruler of your 8th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Jupiter sits in your 3rd house at 14°37' Cancer, which is the sign where it is exalted — at its very strongest. Counting from the 8th house itself, Jupiter stands 8 places along — the 8th from its own house, which is one of the three difficult positions (the 6th, 8th and 12th, called 'dusthana'). The matters of this house are undermined a little from within, and putting them right takes deliberate, sustained effort rather than luck. Because the ruler sits in the 3rd, the affairs of your 8th house get worked out mostly through courage — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 64 out of 100 (strong), so Jupiter is in a position to actually deliver what the 8th house stands for, not merely promise it.
     [3] The ruler, Jupiter, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Pushya, in its 4th quarter. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Jupiter, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] No planet aspects the 8th house. Its affairs run on the ruler's condition alone, with nothing from outside either reinforcing or interfering — outcomes here are quieter and more in your own hands than elsewhere in the chart.
     [5] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 8th house goes rests on Jupiter's condition in the 3rd and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Jupiter — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.

Saturn's aspect on the 10th lands on the house Saturn naturally stands for, and is one of the strongest career glances in the chart: authority through service, seniority through endurance, and a reputation for reliability that compounds. Advancement is slow and essentially irreversible. Coming from a hard planet in an ordinary condition, the aspect keeps this house honest — pressure you learn to work with rather than damage. And because this is the most helpful planet your rising sign has (the yogakaraka), its aspect is one of the best structural supports in your chart wherever it lands.
     [6] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.
     [7] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 10th house goes rests on Saturn's condition in the 4th and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Saturn — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.
  == 11th House — Pisces  occupied=true lord=Ju planets=[Ke] aspects=[Ju:9,Ra:7]
     [1] Your 11th house falls in Pisces. This is the part of the chart that covers gains, income, networks, elder siblings and fulfilment of desires. Ketu sits in it, so the affairs of this house are things you live directly rather than watch from a distance.
     [2] Pisces is ruled by Jupiter, so Jupiter is the ruler of your 11th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Jupiter sits in your 3rd house at 14°37' Cancer, which is the sign where it is exalted — at its very strongest. Counting from the 11th house itself, Jupiter stands 5 places along — the 5th from its own house, which is one of the supportive positions (the corner houses 1, 4, 7, 10 and the fortunate houses 5 and 9). The ruler actively backs what it rules. Because the ruler sits in the 3rd, the affairs of your 11th house get worked out mostly through courage — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 64 out of 100 (strong), so Jupiter is in a position to actually deliver what the 11th house stands for, not merely promise it.
     [3] The ruler, Jupiter, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Pushya, in its 4th quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Saturn, and the flavour is: nourishing, dutiful, deeply auspicious; grows what it protects. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Jupiter, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Ketu sits here at 0°49' Pisces. Ketu rules no sign of its own. It works through the planet that owns the sign it sits in, through the ruler of its star-group (nakshatra), and through whatever it sits beside — a shadow that takes on the colour of its surroundings rather than a house ruler with an agenda of its own. Ketu in the 11th prunes the network: few friends, strange gains, desires that extinguish upon examination. Income arrives through niche or technical channels. The social ladder holds no rungs you care to grip — freedom is the actual profit. Pisces belongs to an enemy of Ketu, so it meets resistance here — a guest whose host does not much like it. Results in this house take roughly twice the usual persistence to arrive. Ketu is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first. One refinement: houses can be counted two ways. By whole signs (used above) Ketu is in the 11th, but by the more precise method that measures from the exact house boundaries (Bhava Chalit), it falls in the 10th. Read its concrete, day-to-day results in the 10th house too; its strength and comfort by sign, described above, stay as they are.
     [5] Ketu falls in the nakshatra Purva Bhadrapada (4th quarter), ruled by Jupiter, whose flavour is: fiery twin-front transformation; intensity behind an austere face. Jupiter, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Ketu, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [6] Jupiter aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 3rd house in Cancer, where it is exalted. By nature Jupiter is a gentle planet (a 'benefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). The aspect landing here is Jupiter's special 9th-house aspect — its blessing on fortune and right living, and the most benevolent aspect there is. Jupiter's glance on the 11th expands gains and fills the network with people of standing; income grows steadily and desires are largely fulfilled. The risk is a portfolio inflated by optimism. Because this gentle planet is itself well placed, its aspect is a genuine protection — something you can rely on when this house is tested. Note the split, though: gentle by nature but working against your rising sign by the houses it rules, so its blessing here comes with strings attached.
     [7] Rahu also aspects this house, from the 5th house in Virgo, in a great friend's sign, retrograde — a hard planet by nature, and neutral by rulership. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Rahu's case direct and obsessive. Rahu's aspect on the 11th is classically excellent for gains — large, unusual, foreign-sourced income and a network far beyond your origins. Desires, however, refill as fast as they are met. A hard planet in a strong position does not merely trouble a house — it also forces you to become competent in its affairs, charging the discipline as its price.
     [8] The house receives 1 gentle and 1 hard aspect — a contested area whose results swing depending on which of these planets is running its main period (dasha) at the time.
  == 12th House — Aries  occupied=false lord=Ma planets=[] aspects=[Su:7,Ve:7]
     [1] Your 12th house falls in Aries. This is the part of the chart that covers expenditure, losses, foreign lands, isolation, sleep and moksha. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Aries is ruled by Mars, so Mars is the ruler of your 12th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Mars sits in your 7th house at 6°43' Scorpio, which is its own sign, so it is on home ground. Counting from the 12th house itself, Mars stands 8 places along — the 8th from its own house, which is one of the three difficult positions (the 6th, 8th and 12th, called 'dusthana'). The matters of this house are undermined a little from within, and putting them right takes deliberate, sustained effort rather than luck. Because the ruler sits in the 7th, the affairs of your 12th house get worked out mostly through marriage — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 58 out of 100 (moderate). Mars can carry the 12th house, but results here follow your own effort closely rather than being handed to you.
     [3] The ruler, Mars, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Anuradha, in its 2nd quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Saturn, and the flavour is: loyal, disciplined devotion; friendship as a spiritual force. Saturn, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Mars, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Sun aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 6th house in Libra, where it is debilitated. By nature Sun is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet that works in your favour by the houses it rules (a 'functional benefic'). The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts. The Sun's gaze on the 12th spends the ego's fuel on distant, private or institutional ends — foreign work, seclusion, hospitals, or genuine spiritual withdrawal. Expenditure is dignified rather than wasteful, but vitality and sleep need guarding. A hard planet in a weak position troubles the house without building much capability in return: the friction comes with little compensating discipline. Its rulerships soften this considerably — a hard planet that works for your rising sign behaves far better than its reputation.
     [5] Venus also aspects this house, from the 6th house in Libra, in its own sign, retrograde — a gentle planet by nature, and neutral by rulership. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts. Venus's aspect on the 12th spends on comfort, beauty and private pleasures, and gives the classical blessing on everything the 12th stands for — bed comforts, foreign residence, and the surrender that mature devotion requires. Discretion is the discipline. Because this gentle planet is itself well placed, its aspect is a genuine protection — something you can rely on when this house is tested.
     [6] The house receives 1 gentle and 1 hard aspect — a contested area whose results swing depending on which of these planets is running its main period (dasha) at the time.
     [7] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 12th house goes rests on Mars's condition in the 7th and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Mars — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.

  == The marriage houses and significators
     [1] Marriage is read from your 7th house and from the planets that naturally stand for partnership. Below are the two deeper views the tradition adds to that: the marriage pada, which describes the institution, and the Navamsa, which describes the daily life inside it.
     [2] Your Upapada Lagna (the marriage pada — the arudha of the 12th house) falls in Gemini, and Mercury rules it, sitting in your 7th house, in an enemy's sign. This point describes marriage as a standing institution in your life rather than as romance — the household, the in-laws, the public fact of being married. Its condition is the best single indicator of how settled and respected that arrangement tends to feel from the inside.
     [3] Your Navamsa (the D-9 divisional chart of marriage and inner strength) is where married life is actually read, and it says this: the Navamsa rising sign is Pisces, ruled by Jupiter; its 7th house falls in Virgo, with Moon, Mars, Saturn there; and Venus lands in Taurus. Treat this as the private texture of the marriage — what the two of you are like at home on an ordinary Tuesday, rather than what the relationship looks like to everyone else.

  == Mangal Dosha
     [1] Mars sits in your 7th house, which technically forms Mangal Dosha from the Lagna and from the Moon — but your chart cancels it: Mars stands in its own sign (Scorpio) — a dignified Mars is held not to afflict; Jupiter aspects Mars — the classical benefic override. That means the heat is there without the affliction the tradition warns about. In daily life it reads as directness rather than damage: you say the difficult thing early, which is usually kinder than the alternative.
  == What your partner tends to be like
     [1] Because Mars rules your 7th house, the person you marry tends to carry that planet's nature — you are drawn to it, and you recognise it quickly. Tradition also reads a direction from this, South of your birthplace, but hold that one loosely: direction rules are the softest technique on this page.
     [2] There are no strong foreign markers on your marriage axis, which leans the reading toward a partner from a familiar cultural circle. That is a tendency in the chart, not a boundary on your life — plenty of these charts marry across every kind of distance.
     [3] Mercury, as your spouse significator, colours their temperament more than anything else here. Look for a strong Mercury signature in whom you actually choose — it is usually more visible in how they behave under stress than in how they present at first.
  == What tradition offers, and what actually helps
     [1] The standard literature recommends strengthening Venus in the traditional way — Friday observances, white clothing, courtesy toward your partner. The last of those is the one that carries the weight: attention paid to the relationship is what the practice is really training.

--- section: cautions ---
  headline: 7 areas in your chart deserve conscious handling rather than luck. Each one below comes with something practical to do about it, and none of them is a verdict.
  score=37 verdict=none confidence=75
  == How to read this
     [1] These are tendencies, not sentences, and none of them is a verdict on your life. Classical astrology lists them for one reason: so you can steer. That is why every caution below arrives with the thing that actually helps — a caution without a counter-measure is just anxiety with a citation.
     [2] Nothing here is medical, legal or financial advice, and none of it should stand in for a professional who can look at your actual situation.

--- section: lucky ---
  headline: The planets working in your favour are Venus, Saturn, Mercury — which gives you numbers 6, 8, 2, the colour white / pastel, and West to face.
  score=none verdict=none confidence=72
  == Where these come from
     [1] From your chart: the ruler of your rising sign (Venus), your yogakaraka (Saturn), the ruler of your Moon's nakshatra (Saturn) and your strongest supportive planet (Mercury) are the ones working in your favour. Their numbers, colours, days and directions are what this section calls yours.
     [2] From your birth date: Moolank 2 and Bhagyank 2, and a name number of 1 on the chaldean system, with a Kua number of 2 — each of which tradition assigns to a planet.
     [3] Treat all of this the way tradition actually intends it: as a set of preferences that tilt the odds slightly and cost nothing to follow, not as rules that decide outcomes. Wearing your colour to an interview is worth about as much as the confidence it gives you, which is not nothing.

--- yogas ---
  [budhaditya] Budhaditya Yoga  planets=Su,Me
      Sun and Mercury conjoin in the 3rd house: sharp administrative intellect and articulate authority, though Mercury's combustion demands you learn to separate ego from analysis.
  [chandra-mangala] Chandra-Mangala Yoga  planets=Mo,Ma
      Moon and Mars conjoin — an earning combination: entrepreneurial drive fused with instinct. Wealth through self-effort, with a temper that needs channels, not suppression.
  [raja-Sa-Ve] Raja Yoga (Kendra–Trikona link)  planets=Ve,Sa
      Venus (an angle lord) and Saturn (a trine lord) stand in mutual aspect. The classical engine of rank: when the ruler of a fortune house (1st, 5th or 9th) joins the ruler of a corner house (1st, 4th, 7th or 10th), status is lifted, and the results ripen in the main periods (dashas) of the two planets involved.
  [dhana-9-11] Dhana Yoga (9th–11th lords)  planets=Me,Su
      The 9th lord Mercury joins the 11th lord Sun — fortune's lord touching the wealth axis. Money arrives through merit, timing or backing rather than grind alone.
  [parivartana-Ju-Me] Parivartana Yoga (Dainya)  planets=Ju,Me
      Jupiter and Mercury sit in each other's signs — a parivartana, or mutual exchange, linking the 3rd and 6th with the 9th and 12th. An exchange fuses two houses: neither can be read without the other, and whatever happens to one shows up in the other. A difficult house is inside the exchange, so its results arrive the hard way — obstruction first, resolution afterwards. The tradition still counts it a yoga: what it costs early it tends to return as capability later.
  [kemadruma] Kemadruma Yoga (cancelled)  planets=Mo
      The Moon has no planetary neighbour in the signs beside it — Kemadruma forms — but it is cancelled because planets occupy the corner houses counted from the Moon. The isolation shows only as an occasional need for solitude, not as the classical loneliness of the yoga.
  [amala-moon] Amala Yoga  planets=Ve
      Only the gentle planets (Venus) occupy the 10th from the Moon. The "stainless" yoga: reputation stays clean, work is respected, and standing survives controversy. Career fortunes rise on merit rather than manoeuvre.

--- personality ---
  headline: Libra rising, Aquarius Moon in Shatabhisha, Sagittarius Sun — a personality led by Moon and tested through Jupiter.
  strongest=Mo weakest=Ju
  == Your rising sign — how you come across
     [1] Your rising sign (the 'Lagna' — the sign that was coming up over the eastern horizon at the moment you were born) is Libra, at 0°49'. The rising sign describes how you come across, the body you live in, and the way you start things. With Libra rising, the outward temperament is gracious, balancing and relationship-oriented; you think by consulting. Charm is effortless, decisiveness is not.
     [2] The rising degree also falls in a nakshatra — one of the 27 star-groups the zodiac is divided into, each with its own character — called Chitra, in its 3rd quarter. That nakshatra colours the body and the first impression you make: brilliant, architectural vision; polishes form into beauty.
     [3] Every sign has a ruling planet, and the ruler of your rising sign — Venus — is the planet that stands for you personally in the chart. It sits in your 2nd house in Scorpio, which is a friend's sign, where it is comfortable; it is also combust — so close to the Sun that its own light is drowned out. Its overall strength (a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors) comes to 30 out of 100 (weak). Because it sits in the 2nd, the centre of gravity of your life leans toward wealth: that is the agenda that colours everything you attempt, and your sense of who you are is bound up in it.
     [4] Venus itself falls in the nakshatra Jyeshtha, in its 4th quarter, whose ruling planet is Mercury. The flavour of that nakshatra is: eldest-born intensity; protective authority won through trials. Mercury, the nakshatra's ruler, is a friend of Venus, so this quality comes through warmly and is easy for others to read.
     [5] For Libra rising, Saturn is the yogakaraka — it rules your 4th house of home and your 5th house of intelligence, giving a patient, structural rise. Mercury, ruling your 9th house of fortune, carries your luck. Jupiter (your 3rd and 6th) is the chief planet working against you; Mars rules your 2nd and 7th, the two houses the tradition watches for health.
  == Your Moon — the mind and its moods
     [1] In Vedic astrology the Moon stands for the mind: your moods, your instincts, what you need in order to feel safe. Your Moon is in Aquarius in your 5th house, which is a friend's sign, where it is comfortable; its overall strength comes to 47 out of 100 (moderate). With the Moon in Aquarius, the mind is detached and observational, feeling at one remove and more comfortable with humanity than with individuals. Emotional independence is real, occasionally to the point of remoteness.
     [2] The Moon falls in the nakshatra Shatabhisha, in its 3rd quarter, whose ruling planet is Rahu. The flavour of that nakshatra is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. This is the single most personal signature in your chart: it sets the sequence of your planetary periods (the Vimshottari dasha, the timing system used throughout this app), and it describes how you actually experience your own life from the inside. Rahu, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Moon, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [3] Because the Moon sits in your 5th house, your emotional life revolves around intelligence. That is where you go for comfort, and it is also where you are most easily unsettled.
  == Your Sun — the will and the core self
     [1] The Sun stands for your will, your sense of self, and the things you need to be respected for. Your Sun is in Sagittarius in your 3rd house, which is a sign that is neutral to it — neither helped nor hindered; its overall strength comes to 43 out of 100 (weak). With the Sun in Sagittarius, the will is principled and expansive: your identity rests on beliefs held, defended and taught.
     [2] Because the Sun sits in your 3rd house, your identity is staked on courage. This is the arena in which you need to matter, and a setback here feels like a failure of self rather than of circumstance.
     [3] The Sun falls in the nakshatra Mula, in its 2nd quarter, whose ruling planet is Ketu. The flavour of that nakshatra is: root-cutting inquiry; destroys to find foundational truth. Ketu, the nakshatra's ruler, is neutral toward Sun, so this quality comes through plainly — neither boosted nor blocked.
  == Your strongest and weakest planets
     [1] Each planet in your chart has been scored for overall strength out of 100 — a combination of the sign it is in, the house, the aspects it receives, and a few other classical factors. Moon is your strongest planet at 47 (moderate), sitting in your 5th house in Aquarius. Its qualities are the ones you reach for by default and perform best with under pressure — whatever Moon stands for is where you are genuinely, reliably competent. The biggest things in its favour are: Dignity: Friend's Sign (Mitra); House placement (5); Aspect from Jupiter.
     [2] Jupiter is your weakest at 24 (afflicted), in your 9th house in Gemini. This is the chart's soft flank: whatever Jupiter stands for is where you tend to over-compensate, avoid, or quietly hand off to other people. The biggest things weighing on it are: Dignity: Great Enemy's Sign (Adhi Shatru); Aspect from Sun; Aspect from Mercury.
     [3] A lot of your personality is the negotiation between these two. Growth, for this chart, looks less like adding to Moon — which needs no help — and more like refusing to let Jupiter's weakness set the terms, because that is precisely the area you are most tempted to route around.
  == Atmakaraka — what this life is for
     [1] Venus is your Atmakaraka — literally the 'soul significator', the planet that has travelled furthest through its sign (29°32'), which the Jaimini school of astrology treats as the planet carrying the deepest purpose of the life. In simple terms: whatever Venus stands for — placed in your 2nd house in Scorpio — is the lesson this life keeps returning to, in relationships, in work, everywhere. It is less "what you do" and more "what you cannot avoid becoming good at".
     [2] Its Karakamsa — the sign the Atmakaraka occupies in the Navamsa, the ninth-division chart — is Pisces: the inner room where that lesson is actually worked out. A Pisces Karakamsa colours the soul's work as impressionable, compassionate and porous to atmosphere, absorbing whatever surrounds you and needing boundaries you are reluctant to build. Imagination and faith are the standing strengths.
  == Navamsa — the inner person
     [1] The Navamsa (D-9) is a second chart derived from your birth chart by dividing each sign into nine parts. Tradition reads it for the inner person and for marriage, and treats it as the test of whether what the birth chart promises actually holds. Its rising sign is Libra. If the birth chart is how life presents itself, the Navamsa is how you are on the inside once the noise settles — and a Libra inner nature is gracious, balancing and relationship-oriented; you think by consulting. Charm is effortless, decisiveness is not. When the outer sign (Libra) and inner sign (Libra) differ in element, people who only know you casually often misread you; those close to you meet the Navamsa.
     [2] Moon, Mars, Saturn are 'Vargottama' — in the same sign in both the birth chart and the Navamsa. A Vargottama planet keeps its promises: what it shows on the surface is what it actually is underneath, and it holds steady under pressure.
  == Aspects on your rising sign — who edits the personality
     [1] Planets influence houses they do not sit in by 'aspecting' them — casting their influence across the chart to particular houses. Every planet aspects the 7th house from itself, and Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Rahu and Ketu each have extra special aspects. The following planets aspect your rising sign, and each one edits the personality others meet:
     [2] Jupiter aspects your rising sign from your 9th house — Jupiter's special 5th-house aspect — its blessing on intelligence, good counsel and children.
     [3] Rahu aspects your rising sign from your 9th house — Rahu's special 5th-house aspect — an amplifier of intelligence and of desire.
     [4] Each of these adds its own colour on top of the rising sign itself. If people describe you in ways that do not quite match the rising-sign portrait above, one of these planets is usually why.
  == How you are seen vs how you are (Arudha Lagna)
     [1] The Arudha Lagna is the point in the chart that stands for your public image — how the world perceives you, as distinct from how you actually are. Yours falls in Sagittarius, while your actual rising sign is Libra. So the public image runs on Sagittarius's wavelength — expansive, principled and restless for meaning, needing both a horizon and a doctrine — while the person underneath operates as Libra. The gap is not dishonesty; it is simply that your reputation forms around the themes of your 3rd house. Knowing which conversations are about the image and which are about you is a lifelong advantage.
  == Yoga signatures in the personality
     [1] A 'yoga' is a named combination of planets that the classical texts single out as producing a specific result. 2 combinations in your chart stamp themselves directly on the character: Budhaditya Yoga, Chandra-Mangala Yoga.
     [2] Budhaditya Yoga — Sun and Mercury conjoin in the 3rd house: sharp administrative intellect and articulate authority, though Mercury's combustion demands you learn to separate ego from analysis.
     [3] Chandra-Mangala Yoga — Moon and Mars conjoin — an earning combination: entrepreneurial drive fused with instinct. Wealth through self-effort, with a temper that needs channels, not suppression.
  == Which planets work for you, and which against
     [1] Which planets help you and which hinder you is not fixed — it depends on your rising sign, because the rising sign decides which houses each planet rules. A planet that rules good houses for Libra rising works in your favour whatever its natural reputation; one that rules difficult houses works against you even if it is a 'good' planet in general.
     [2] Saturn is the yogakaraka for Libra rising — the single most helpful planet your rising sign can have, because it rules one of the corner houses and one of the fortunate houses at the same time. Saturn — in your 8th house, in a sign neutral to it, strength 30 out of 100 (weak). Its condition is the best single predictor of how far your personality converts into standing in the world.
     [3] Working for you: Saturn — in your 8th house, in a sign neutral to it, strength 30 out of 100 (weak); Mercury — in your 3rd house, in an enemy's sign, strength 24 out of 100 (afflicted); Venus — in your 2nd house, in a friend's sign, strength 30 out of 100 (weak). The traits these planets govern are the ones you can lean on without second-guessing, and their main periods (dashas) feel like the personality working as designed.
     [4] Working against you: Jupiter — in your 9th house, in a great enemy's sign, strength 24 out of 100 (afflicted); Sun — in your 3rd house, in a sign neutral to it, strength 43 out of 100 (weak); Mars — in your 5th house, in a friend's sign, strength 47 out of 100 (moderate). These are not evil planets — they are the ones whose house rulerships pull against your rising sign's interests. Where one of them is strong, you have a talent that repeatedly gets you into trouble; where weak, a blind spot you rarely notice.
     [5] Neutral: Moon — in your 5th house, in a friend's sign, strength 47 out of 100 (moderate). These take their colour from whatever they sit with rather than carrying an agenda of their own.

  == 1st House — Libra  occupied=false lord=Ve planets=[] aspects=[Ju:5,Ra:5]
     [1] Your 1st house falls in Libra. This is the part of the chart that covers self, body, vitality, temperament and the overall direction of life. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Libra is ruled by Venus, so Venus is the ruler of your 1st house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Venus sits in your 2nd house at 29°32' Scorpio, which is a friend's sign, where it is comfortable. It is also combust (so close to the Sun that its light is drowned out). Counting from the 1st house itself, Venus stands 2 places along — the 2nd from its own house, a workable but unremarkable position that neither helps nor hurts. Because the ruler sits in the 2nd, the affairs of your 1st house get worked out mostly through wealth — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 30 out of 100 (weak), which is low. The 1st house is under-resourced at its ruler, and its affairs need conscious support from you before they hold weight.
     [3] The ruler, Venus, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Jyeshtha, in its 4th quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Mercury, and the flavour is: eldest-born intensity; protective authority won through trials. Mercury, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Venus, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [4] Jupiter aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 9th house in Gemini, in a great enemy's sign, retrograde. By nature Jupiter is a gentle planet (a 'benefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). The aspect landing here is Jupiter's special 5th-house aspect — its blessing on intelligence, good counsel and children. Jupiter's drishti on the Lagna is among the finest protections in a chart: an optimistic, ethical temperament, a well-made body, and a habit of landing on one's feet. Weight, literal and metaphorical, is the only excess. The good intent is there, but the planet itself is poorly placed, so the protection is real yet thin, and tends to arrive later than you expect. Note the split, though: gentle by nature but working against your rising sign by the houses it rules, so its blessing here comes with strings attached.
     [5] Rahu also aspects this house, from the 9th house in Gemini, in a sign neutral to it, retrograde — a hard planet by nature, and neutral by rulership. The aspect landing here is Rahu's special 5th-house aspect — an amplifier of intelligence and of desire. Rahu's drishti on the Lagna amplifies the persona and blurs its edges: unusual magnetism, an unconventional self-presentation, and an identity assembled rather than inherited. Other people's projections stick to you easily. Coming from a hard planet in an ordinary condition, the aspect keeps this house honest — pressure you learn to work with rather than damage.
     [6] The house receives 1 gentle and 1 hard aspect — a contested area whose results swing depending on which of these planets is running its main period (dasha) at the time.
     [7] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 1st house goes rests on Venus's condition in the 2nd and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Venus — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.
  == 2nd House — Scorpio  occupied=true lord=Ma planets=[Ve] aspects=[Sa:7]
     [1] Your 2nd house falls in Scorpio. This is the part of the chart that covers wealth, family, speech, food and accumulated resources. Venus sits in it, so the affairs of this house are things you live directly rather than watch from a distance.
     [2] Scorpio is ruled by Mars, so Mars is the ruler of your 2nd house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Mars sits in your 5th house at 15°00' Aquarius, which is a friend's sign, where it is comfortable. Counting from the 2nd house itself, Mars stands 4 places along — the 4th from its own house, which is one of the supportive positions (the corner houses 1, 4, 7, 10 and the fortunate houses 5 and 9). The ruler actively backs what it rules. Because the ruler sits in the 5th, the affairs of your 2nd house get worked out mostly through intelligence — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 47 out of 100 (moderate). Mars can carry the 2nd house, but results here follow your own effort closely rather than being handed to you.
     [3] The ruler, Mars, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Shatabhisha, in its 3rd quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Rahu, and the flavour is: veiled healing; solitary depths, mysticism and medicine. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Mars, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] Venus sits here at 29°32' Scorpio. For your Libra rising sign, Venus rules your 1st and 8th houses — self and longevity — which makes it a planet that works in your favour by the houses it rules (a 'functional benefic'). Venus in the 2nd sweetens wealth and speech alike: money through arts, luxury goods, finance and beauty industries. The voice may literally be musical. Family life is refined; the palate is expensive and knows it. Scorpio belongs to a friend of Venus, so the placement is comfortable and cooperates with you rather than resisting. Venus is combust — so close to the Sun that its own light is drowned out. Its outward results are weakened while pride and self-image crowd into its territory; the remedy is humility, and deliberately practising the qualities this planet stands for. One refinement: houses can be counted two ways. By whole signs (used above) Venus is in the 2nd, but by the more precise method that measures from the exact house boundaries (Bhava Chalit), it falls in the 3rd. Read its concrete, day-to-day results in the 3rd house too; its strength and comfort by sign, described above, stay as they are.
     [5] Venus falls in the nakshatra Jyeshtha (4th quarter), ruled by Mercury, whose flavour is: eldest-born intensity; protective authority won through trials. Mercury, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Venus, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [6] Saturn aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 8th house in Taurus, in a sign neutral to it, retrograde. By nature Saturn is a hard planet (a 'malefic'), and for your rising sign it is the single most helpful planet your rising sign can have (the 'yogakaraka'), because it rules one of the supporting houses and one of the fortunate houses at the same time. The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Saturn's case direct weight, and the one that delays. Saturn's aspect on the 2nd disciplines wealth and speech alike — accumulation is slow, deliberate and permanent; words are few and load-bearing. Early family life carries constraint, later provision is secure. Coming from a hard planet in an ordinary condition, the aspect keeps this house honest — pressure you learn to work with rather than damage. And because this is the most helpful planet your rising sign has (the yogakaraka), its aspect is one of the best structural supports in your chart wherever it lands.
     [7] Every aspect reaching this house comes from a hard planet. With no gentle planet to balance them, what this house stands for needs conscious defending, and the house tends to mature through difficulty rather than ease — which also means it usually matures well.

     [4] Sun sits here at 5°23' Sagittarius. You out-work siblings and rivals alike, writes and speaks with conviction, and takes initiatives that others only discuss. Younger siblings may live in this person's shadow. Sagittarius is neutral ground for Sun — the sign neither helps nor hinders it. What you get out of this house tracks what you put in almost exactly, with no subsidy and no surcharge.
     [5] Sun falls in the nakshatra Mula (2nd quarter), ruled by Ketu, whose flavour is: root-cutting inquiry; destroys to find foundational truth. Ketu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Sun, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [6] Mercury sits here at 14°27' Sagittarius. For your Libra rising sign, Mercury rules your 9th and 12th houses — fortune and expenditure — which makes it a planet that works in your favour by the houses it rules (a 'functional benefic'). Mercury in the 3rd is Mercury doubled — communication in the house of communication. Media, marketing, coding and commerce all flourish. Siblings are clever company. The mind darts; finishing what is started is the discipline to build. Sagittarius belongs to an enemy of Mercury, so it meets resistance here — a guest whose host does not much like it. Results in this house take roughly twice the usual persistence to arrive. Mercury is combust — so close to the Sun that its own light is drowned out. Its outward results are weakened while pride and self-image crowd into its territory; the remedy is humility, and deliberately practising the qualities this planet stands for.
     [7] Mercury falls in the nakshatra Purva Ashadha (1st quarter), ruled by Venus, whose flavour is: invincible early victories; proud, philosophical, water-blessed. Venus, the ruler of that nakshatra, is a natural friend of Mercury, so this flavour is passed on willingly and arrives in a form you recognise.
     [8] Ketu sits here at 3°03' Sagittarius. Ketu rules no sign of its own. It works through the planet that owns the sign it sits in, through the ruler of its star-group (nakshatra), and through whatever it sits beside — a shadow that takes on the colour of its surroundings rather than a house ruler with an agenda of its own. Ketu in the 3rd gives effortless, headless courage — bravery without the need for applause. Sibling bonds carry past-life residue. Technical intuition (code, machines, mantra) works below the level of explanation. Sagittarius belongs to an enemy of Ketu, so it meets resistance here — a guest whose host does not much like it. Results in this house take roughly twice the usual persistence to arrive. Ketu is retrograde — it appeared to be moving backwards through the sky when you were born. In practice that turns its energy inward and intensifies it: the themes of this house come back around more than once, and matters tend to conclude on the second or third pass rather than the first.
     [9] Ketu falls in the nakshatra Mula (1st quarter), ruled by Ketu, whose flavour is: root-cutting inquiry; destroys to find foundational truth. Ketu sits in a nakshatra it rules itself, so its intent reaches the world undiluted — what it promises, it delivers in its own voice.
     [10] Sun and Ketu both sit in nakshatras ruled by Ketu, which tightens their pairing: they do not merely share a sign, they answer to the same planet, and Ketu's main periods bring both to the surface at once.
     [11] Jupiter aspects this house — that is, it reaches into it from where it sits, the 9th house in Gemini, in a great enemy's sign, retrograde. By nature Jupiter is a gentle planet (a 'benefic'), and for your rising sign it is a planet whose house rulerships pull against your interests, whatever its natural character (a 'functional malefic'). The aspect landing here is the full 7th-house aspect every planet casts — in Jupiter's case a direct protection. Note the split, though: gentle by nature but working against your rising sign by the houses it rules, so its blessing here comes with strings attached.
     [12] Rahu also aspects this house, from the 9th house in Gemini, in a sign neutral to it, retrograde — a hard planet by nature, and neutral by rulership.      [13] The house receives 1 gentle and 1 hard aspect — a contested area whose results swing depending on which of these planets is running its main period (dasha) at the time.
     [14] Sun–Mercury (Budhaditya) welds intellect to authority — administrative brilliance and articulate command. When Mercury sits deep in combustion, cleverness serves ego; with distance, it advises it. Their combined strength is just 28 out of 100 across 9.1°, so this combination is compromised — you will feel its difficulties more reliably than its gifts. Deliberately strengthening Sun is the single most useful thing to do about it.
     [15] Sun–Ketu quietly detaches the ego from its throne — authority exercised without appetite for it. Identity questions run deep, often father-linked. The strength here is incorruptibility: what you never craved cannot be used to buy you. The two planets are 2.3° apart and their combined strength is only 50 out of 100, so they share a house more than a purpose. The promise is real but muted, and tends to surface mainly during Sun's main periods.
     [16] Mercury–Ketu perceives past the syntax: intuitive analysis, mathematical or mystical shortcuts, speech that ends conversations by finishing them. Detail-tracking bores it. Best in research, jyotisha, code and any field where insight outranks paperwork. Their combined strength is just 28 out of 100 across 11.4°, so this combination is compromised — you will feel its difficulties more reliably than its gifts. Deliberately strengthening Ketu is the single most useful thing to do about it.
     [17] With 3 planets sharing one house, it works like a committee: Sun chairs it as the strongest member, the group spans 11.4° and carries a combined strength of 25 out of 100 (afflicted). The crowding is a liability rather than an asset here — the house is overloaded, and what it stands for needs deliberate, sustained repair.

And because this is the most helpful planet your rising sign has (the yogakaraka), its aspect is one of the best structural supports in your chart wherever it lands.
     [11] Rahu also aspects this house, from the 9th house in Gemini, in a sign neutral to it, retrograde — a hard planet by nature, and neutral by rulership. The aspect landing here is Rahu's special 9th-house aspect — a distorting influence on inherited fortune and belief. Rahu's glance on the 5th makes intelligence unconventional and speculative, romance intense and rule-breaking, and matters of children a site of either delay or unusual circumstance. Speculation attracts, and must be governed. Coming from a hard planet in an ordinary condition, the aspect keeps this house honest — pressure you learn to work with rather than damage.
     [12] The house receives 1 gentle and 2 hard aspects — a contested area whose results swing depending on which of these planets is running its main period (dasha) at the time.
     [13] Moon–Mars (Chandra-Mangala) monetises instinct: entrepreneurial reflexes, protective passion, wealth built through decisive emotional intelligence. The same circuitry produces flash-flood tempers — profitable when dammed, destructive when not. At 1.0° apart the two planets are genuinely blended — combined strength 60 out of 100, with Moon the stronger. Expect the combination to show up steadily rather than dramatically, in proportion to the effort you put into it.
  == 6th House — Pisces  occupied=false lord=Ju planets=[] aspects=[]
     [1] Your 6th house falls in Pisces. This is the part of the chart that covers health, debts, enemies, service, litigation and daily discipline. No planet sits in it. That is not a weakness in itself — most houses in most charts are empty. An empty house is read the way the classical texts prescribe: by the condition of the planet that rules it, and by the planets that aspect it from elsewhere.
     [2] Pisces is ruled by Jupiter, so Jupiter is the ruler of your 6th house — the planet whose condition decides how this area of life goes. Jupiter sits in your 9th house at 18°14' Gemini, which is the sign of a great enemy, where it struggles to be itself. It is also retrograde (appearing to move backwards in the sky at your birth). Counting from the 6th house itself, Jupiter stands 4 places along — the 4th from its own house, which is one of the supportive positions (the corner houses 1, 4, 7, 10 and the fortunate houses 5 and 9). The ruler actively backs what it rules. Because the ruler sits in the 9th, the affairs of your 6th house get worked out mostly through fortune — that is where they actually play out in daily life. Its overall strength — a combined score from its sign, house, aspects and other factors — comes to 24 out of 100 (afflicted), which is low. The 6th house is under-resourced at its ruler, and its affairs need conscious support from you before they hold weight.
     [3] The ruler, Jupiter, also falls in the star-group (nakshatra) called Ardra, in its 4th quarter. Each nakshatra has its own ruling planet and its own flavour — here the ruler is Rahu, and the flavour is: stormy, penetrating intellect; breakthroughs after inner turbulence. Rahu, the ruler of that nakshatra, is neutral toward Jupiter, so this flavour comes through as it is — neither boosted nor blocked.
     [4] No planet aspects the 6th house. Its affairs run on the ruler's condition alone, with nothing from outside either reinforcing or interfering — outcomes here are quieter and more in your own hands than elsewhere in the chart.
     [5] In short: with no planet in it to speak for it, how your 6th house goes rests on Jupiter's condition in the 9th and on the aspects described above. Anything that strengthens Jupiter — its main periods, its transits, deliberate attention to what it stands for — improves this house; nothing else reaches it directly.

--- section: marriage ---
  headline: Your marriage indications are mixed: warm significators on one side, some classical delay markers on the other. For a chart like this the windows matter far more than the average does.
  score=55 verdict=none confidence=75
  == The marriage houses and significators
     [1] Marriage is read from your 7th house and from the planets that naturally stand for partnership. Below are the two deeper views the tradition adds to that: the marriage pada, which describes the institution, and the Navamsa, which describes the daily life inside it.
     [2] Your Upapada Lagna (the marriage pada — the arudha of the 12th house) falls in Sagittarius, and Jupiter rules it, sitting in your 9th house, in a great enemy's sign. This point describes marriage as a standing institution in your life rather than as romance — the household, the in-laws, the public fact of being married. Its condition is the best single indicator of how settled and respected that arrangement tends to feel from the inside.
     [3] Your Navamsa (the D-9 divisional chart of marriage and inner strength) is where married life is actually read, and it says this: the Navamsa rising sign is Libra, ruled by Venus; its 7th house falls in Aries, with nothing in it; and Venus lands in Pisces. Treat this as the private texture of the marriage — what the two of you are like at home on an ordinary Tuesday, rather than what the relationship looks like to everyone else.
     why(0) Your 7th house is ruled by Mars, and it sits in your 5th house, in a friend's sign, with an overall strength of 47 out of 100. That is where your partnership life gets carried out — 5th-house matters and your marriage tend to move together.
     why(5) Venus is strong in your Navamsa, the chart that describes married life from the inside. Affection in your marriage tends to deepen with the years instead of wearing thin — the tenderness is still there after the novelty has gone. [BPHS, Ch.6 (Navamsa)]
     why(2) Sun is your Darakaraka (the spouse significator — least advanced by degree), and it sits in your 3rd house. The person you marry tends to carry that planet's signature — its temperament, its pace, its way of handling difficulty — closely enough that the description usually feels familiar once you meet them. [Jaimini Upadesa Sutras, chara karakas]
     why(-4) Venus is your natural significator for marriage, and it sits in your 2nd house, in a friend's sign, too close to the Sun to shine on its own (30/100). A significator under pressure usually shows up as a slower, more deliberate route into partnership — you tend to learn about love the long way, which is not the same as being denied it. [BPHS, kalatra karaka]
     why(-3) Ketu casts its aspect on your 7th house, which the classics read as a "later is better" influence on marriage. In ordinary terms: early partnerships tend to teach rather than last, and the ones you choose after about thirty hold much better. [Phaladeepika, malefic influence on the 7th]
  == Mangal Dosha
     [1] Mars sits in your 5th house, which technically forms Mangal Dosha from the Moon — but your chart cancels it: Jupiter aspects Mars — the classical benefic override. That means the heat is there without the affliction the tradition warns about. In daily life it reads as directness rather than damage: you say the difficult thing early, which is usually kinder than the alternative.
  == What your partner tends to be like
     [1] Because Mars rules your 7th house, the person you marry tends to carry that planet's nature — you are drawn to it, and you recognise it quickly. Tradition also reads a direction from this, South of your birthplace, but hold that one loosely: direction rules are the softest technique on this page.
     [2] There are no strong foreign markers on your marriage axis, which leans the reading toward a partner from a familiar cultural circle. That is a tendency in the chart, not a boundary on your life — plenty of these charts marry across every kind of distance.
     [3] Sun, as your spouse significator, colours their temperament more than anything else here. Look for a strong Sun signature in whom you actually choose — it is usually more visible in how they behave under stress than in how they present at first.
  == What tradition offers, and what actually helps
     [1] The standard literature recommends strengthening Venus in the traditional way — Friday observances, white clothing, courtesy toward your partner. The last of those is the one that carries the weight: attention paid to the relationship is what the practice is really training.

  == Three different lives abroad, scored separately
     [1] Travelling often, living abroad for years, and settling permanently are three different things, and a chart can be strong for one and quiet on the others. They are scored independently here for that reason.
     [2] These are tendencies rather than certainties. A strong score means the chart supports that shape of life and it tends to come easily; a low one means it costs more effort, not that it is closed to you.
     [3] Open any row to see what moved its number. A ▲ is a signature that pulls you abroad, a ▼ is something that holds you where you are. A low score with only ▲ reasons under it simply means few of the classical signatures are present — nothing in the chart is pushing back.
     item travel score=64 verdict=Supportive :: Travelling often — trips, assignments, pilgrimages, and back home again
        why(5) Your 12th house (distant lands) and your 3rd house (short trips) are tied together: Mercury (your 12th) and Jupiter (your 3rd) sit together or in each other's houses. This reads as a lot of going and coming rather than one big move: frequent flights, short stints, work that keeps you in motion.
        why(6) Jupiter, Rahu occupy your 9th house of long journeys and higher learning, so travel in your life tends to have a purpose attached — study, teaching, belief, or something you went to find.
        why(4) Sun, Mercury, Ketu occupy your 3rd house of short journeys, which reads as frequent movement rather than distant movement — a life with a lot of trips in it.

        why(6) Rahu, the planet of foreign places and of everything unfamiliar, sits in your 9th house. Wherever Rahu falls is where you are willing to be a beginner in a strange place — and that willingness is most of what migration actually asks for.
        why(6) Rahu aspects Moon (your Moon) and Mercury (the ruler of your 12th house of distant lands). An aspect is a lighter touch than sitting together, but it is the same message: the planet of the unfamiliar has a hand on the things that stand for you, your mind, your home or the far-away place — and where Rahu presses, the familiar option tends not to be the one taken.
        why(4) Mercury, the ruler of your 12th house, sits with Ketu. Saturn, Rahu and Ketu are the three planets most associated with distance, separation and the unfamiliar; any of them keeping company with the ruler of the house of distant lands colours that house toward a long time away rather than a short one.

     [1] The foreign houses in your chart point at study or teaching — going to learn something, or to pass it on. That is usually the thread worth following when an opportunity appears and you are trying to judge whether it is the one.
  == Direction — the softest reading here
     [1] Tradition reads a direction from your strongest foreign significator, which is Rahu: South-West of your birthplace. Hold this one loosely. Direction rules are the weakest technique on this page, and no sensible decision about where to live should turn on them — if a good opportunity points the other way, take the opportunity.

--- section: cautions ---
  headline: 9 areas in your chart deserve conscious handling rather than luck. Each one below comes with something practical to do about it, and none of them is a verdict.
  score=33 verdict=none confidence=60
  caveat: Shadbala, the classical strength measure, needs an exact birth time and place, so the weak-planet cautions here fall back on the quicker composite score. They are still worth reading; they are just less precisely graded.
  == How to read this
     [1] These are tendencies, not sentences, and none of them is a verdict on your life. Classical astrology lists them for one reason: so you can steer. That is why every caution below arrives with the thing that actually helps — a caution without a counter-measure is just anxiety with a citation.
     [2] Nothing here is medical, legal or financial advice, and none of it should stand in for a professional who can look at your actual situation.

--- section: lucky ---
  headline: The planets working in your favour are Venus, Saturn, Rahu, Mercury — which gives you numbers 6, 8, 3, the colour white / pastel, and South-West to face.
  score=none verdict=none confidence=64
  == Where these come from
     [1] From your chart: the ruler of your rising sign (Venus), your yogakaraka (Saturn), the ruler of your Moon's nakshatra (Rahu) and your strongest supportive planet (Mercury) are the ones working in your favour. Their numbers, colours, days and directions are what this section calls yours.
     [2] From your birth date: Moolank 3 and Bhagyank 9, and a name number of 3 on the chaldean system, with a Kua number of 8 — each of which tradition assigns to a planet.
     [3] Treat all of this the way tradition actually intends it: as a set of preferences that tilt the odds slightly and cost nothing to follow, not as rules that decide outcomes. Wearing your colour to an interview is worth about as much as the confidence it gives you, which is not nothing.
